I've traced it. Here's what's actually going on — and the short version is **nothing is blocking Fable 5 on your account; what you're hitting is a usage warning, not an access denial.**

## What I confirmed

**Fable 5 is fully enabled for you.** Your account (`<owner-email>`, org role *admin*, plan `claude_max` / `default_claude_max_20x`) has Fable explicitly turned on:
- `/root/.claude.json` → `clientDataCache.cedar_lagoon` = `{claude-fable: true, claude-mythos: true}`
- `additionalModelOptionsCache` lists `claude-fable-5[1m]` ("Fable") as a selectable model
- Your global default is already `claude-fable-5[1m]` (`/root/.claude/settings.json`), and `CLAUDE_EFFORT=max` is live, with `unpinFable5LaunchEffort: true` (max effort allowed on Fable)

So this is **not** an entitlement/eligibility flag, and I ruled out the things that *would* force you off a model: no enterprise `managed-settings.json` (checked all three paths — absent), no `hooks` anywhere, and no safety/content-flag entries in `gotty.log` or `daemon.log`.

## The actual cause of the "flag"

That model's own description is the tell: Fable 5 *"Uses your limits ~2× faster than Opus."* Combine that with two facts on your account:

1. **Extra usage is disabled at the org level** — `oauthAccount.hasExtraUsageEnabled: false`, `cachedExtraUsageDisabledReason: "org_level_disabled"`. So there's **no pay-as-you-go overflow** past your plan limits — hitting the cap is a hard wall, not a soft one.
2. You run **huge parallel-agent workloads** — the SuperGrok1.5 project alone logged ~450k + ~900k output tokens on Opus 4.8 (~$82 of equivalent usage).

So every time you select Fable (especially for a swarm of agents), Claude Code warns you that it burns your Max-20× limits ~2× faster with no overflow to catch the overrun. That recurring warning is almost certainly the "flag" you keep seeing. *(I can't see your screen — if the exact wording is different, tell me and I'll re-trace.)*

## One more thing that's silently fighting your "Fable for agents" intent

Separate from the warning: your SuperGrok1.5 project pins **subagents to Opus**, regardless of your Fable default —

- `/workspace/SuperGrok1.5/.claude/settings.local.json` → `env.CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL: "opus"`

This is a leftover from your earlier sessions, where you repeatedly instructed *"all agents fixed to use opus 4.8 1M at max effort"* (it's in your command history). It overrides the global Fable default for any agent that isn't explicitly pinned — even though you also have a `fable-worker` agent defined (`model: fable`). So whenever you work inside that repo, "use Fable for all agents" won't hold until that line is changed. (Note: this session is rooted at `/`, not the repo, so that pin isn't active *right now* — but it bites the moment the sweep operates on SuperGrok1.5.)

Here's the straight answer.

**Verdict: you're good to keep Fable 5 as your model in general — but you can't rely on it for *this repo's* heavy requests.** Two separate things:

- *Account / model access:* nothing is blocking you. Fable 5 is enabled, you're talking to me on it right now, and it's the correct choice for genuinely hard work elsewhere. Go ahead.
- *This codebase specifically:* the competing-model classifier will keep firing on requests that pull SuperGrok's content into context. That's not a config you can flip — it's a server-side AUP classifier that's part of Fable's extra dual-use layer, which Opus 4.8 doesn't carry. So on this repo, Fable is a recurring wall, not an occasional hiccup.

And yes — the error text *did* give me real context. It confirmed my second read (content classifier, not limits/config) and it matches a well-documented Claude Code failure mode. The issue tracker is full of it: legitimate work getting hit with this exact "violates our Usage Policy" block, and — importantly for you — **once it fires it can poison the rest of the session**, so even benign follow-ups start getting blocked until you start fresh. The community also reports the official appeal path is slow-to-unreliable for individual developers, so I won't oversell "just report it and it's fixed."

Note the unpredictability cuts both ways: I just read your entire HANDOFF/README/audit-notes on Fable in this session without tripping it, yet your request did. It's probabilistic on request content — which is exactly why it's unusable for *reliable* heavy work here.

**So, how to keep Fable in your workflow (the "if not, how"):**

1. **Per-task model switching — the practical one.** Keep Fable 5 as your default driver, and `/model` down to **Opus 4.8 1M** only for the SuperGrok-heavy operations (full sweeps, anything that loads the "model training stack" framing). Opus is a frontier model and literally built this entire repo — it's not a meaningful downgrade for kernel/optimizer work, and it has zero classifier friction here. You lose nothing on capability for *this* codebase.
2. **Start a fresh session if a block poisons the current one** — the contamination is documented; a new session clears the accumulated context.
3. **Report the false positive** with Request ID `req_011Cbvmwr17TRQYQgeYVzVJn` (you're org admin, so you can file it) — worth doing, but treat it as the slow track, not your unblock.
4. **Mythos 5** is the real long-term unlock: same underlying model *without* the dual-use layer, for approved organizations. If your work needs Fable-tier capability on exactly this kind of content, applying for that access through Anthropic is the sanctioned fix rather than fighting the classifier.

One caveat I'll hold the line on: I won't help you reword prompts or rename things specifically to slip past the classifier — that's evasion of a safety control. The earlier point that the "SuperGrok" name and "model training stack" framing are the likely triggers is just diagnosis; what you do with your own project's naming is your call.

My recommendation for right now: run the sweep you originally asked for on **Opus 4.8 1M** (reliable, no blocks), keep Fable as your everyday driver, and file the report in parallel. - **HANDOFF lists neuralgrok as "done across models," but it's eager on all 3.** Its kernel is built and even in the routing sets, but the race deliberately doesn't take the L3 path because its amplifier MLP trains host-side on gradients the megakernel doesn't return ("kernel-ready, race-blocked").

**The 16 blocked cells, strictly by tractability:**
- **muon/mamba (1)** — proven port: vit/decoder already do Muon's in-kernel Newton-Schulz; mamba just needs the same P2.7 block + a 13-entry 2D-weight table. High confidence, one rebuild. *This is the clean next win.*
- **decoder/muon + mamba/prodigy** — already converted and **passing live** (they're inside the 17); they're just sitting **uncommitted** in the working tree. Need committing, not building.
- **neuralgrok ×3** — decision-gated, not a clean win: forcing it onto L3-TC means running the megakernel *and* a separate eager fwd/bwd to feed the host amplifier — double-computing the model. That's a "make the metric green" move the project's own *no-suppression* rule should veto unless we re-architect the amplifier.
- **looksam ×3 + SG11 ×3 + SG15 ×3 (9)** — all blocked on the *same* missing piece: an **in-kernel SAM second forward+backward** (perturb weights → recompute grad). Build that mechanism once, all nine unblock. Multi-day. And the honest caveat: **SG11/SG15 will show wgmma but won't grok** — they're parity-correct yet memorize-then-collapse (documented dynamics, not a kernel bug).
- **SG2 ×3** — hardest: needs the SAM 2nd-pass *plus* an in-kernel segmented sort *plus* composing a ~1200-line meta-net into the L3 codegen, and it won't grok until a separate CSA/HCA indexer-fidelity gap is fixed. Defer behind everything.

### ASSISTANT [2026-06-11T06:43:18]
Stage-0 probe came back clean: `compile.py` is a genuine autotuning compiler (Bayesian/exhaustive search, PGO, split AOT/JIT, per-(opt,model,arch), cost-model, pruners — far past raw nvcc), there's a 108-object + `build.ninja` incremental base ready to fast-path, and I pinned the roofline formula from `tuning/roofline.py` + ROOFLINE.md. That last bit is exactly what makes your question answerable with hard numbers — so let me go straight at it, because it's the most important thing you've raised.

## Yes — it's true, and decisively so. The model scale is the limiter, not the kernels.

Your own roofline data settles it. On an H100 the bf16 tensor-core ceiling is **989 TF/s**. Measured achieved throughput at d=128:

- megakernel: decoder **1.25**, vit **1.23**, mamba **0.85** TF/s → ~**0.1% of peak**
- even hand-tuned eager cuBLAS tops out at decoder 8.45, vit **21.0**, mamba 1.31 → ~**2% of peak** at best

*Nothing* here is close to peak — because the GEMMs are tiny. The roofline % is `achieved_FLOPs ÷ (wall × peak)`, and with d=128 the matmuls (128×128-ish) are far too small to feed the tensor cores; they sit mostly idle no matter how good the kernel is. That's physics, not a kernel defect.

**Batch is not the lever, and yes — you overshot.** The data shows the megakernel saturates at **B≈2,048** (142k samples/s) and then *declines*: at B=16,384 it's −3.5%, at 32,768 it's −10%. It's occupancy-pinned at one CTA/SM, so a bigger batch just adds grid-stride passes with no new parallelism. You're running 8× past the knee. ROOFLINE.md states it flatly: *"the path to a higher fraction is multi-CTA-per-tensor tiling, NOT a larger batch."*

**The killer point for the loop we just designed:** at this scale, your own pass already found **zero header levers produced a measurable before/after** — the PIPE_DEPTH probe compiled byte-identical. So the recursive "tweak → measure roofline → keep if better" loop *cannot move the number at d=128* — there's nothing to optimize because one CTA already holds the whole tiny tensor. Scaling the model isn't cosmetic; it's the **prerequisite** for the optimization loop to do anything at all.

And here's why it's the *right* work, not a cheat: at d≈2048 a layer no longer fits in one CTA, so the one-CTA-per-SM megakernel **must** be redesigned into a real multi-CTA tiled GEMM pipeline (TMA, deep cp.async, swizzle). That redesign is exactly where the roofline climbs **and** where genuine kernel engineering lives. Scaling the model and doing the structural optimization are the same project.

## How big

The master knob is `d_model` (hidden dim), because GEMM efficiency scales with it. Rough H100 bf16 targets:

- **d=512** → ~20–35% of peak (minimum to see the needle move)
- **d=1024** → ~40–60% (cheaper per run)
- **d=2048, ff=8192, ~12–24 layers, seq 1024–2048** → ~**55–75%** — a legit "small LLM on an H100" benchmark, where kernel quality genuinely sets the number. **This is my recommendation.**
- mamba will always trail the transformers — its selective scan is memory-bound, so its win is VRAM (already shown ~3.6×), not FLOP fraction. Honest caveat, not a bug.

## My recommendation: decouple the two

Keep the **grokking race tiny** (that's the actual science — over-parameterizing "a÷b mod 97" would destroy the grokking dynamics you're studying). Add a separate **roofline-benchmark config at d≈2048** built from the *same portable components* — which also makes your portability claim concrete (the components run at real scale). Run the Stage-3 optimization loop against *that*, where "maximal" is a meaningful, climbable target instead of an inert one.

On billing: a d=2048 run costs more each than d=128 — but d=128 runs are *worthless* for the loop (provably inert), so spending on meaningful measurements is the cost-effective choice, not the expensive one. And at scale you won't need huge batches (each sample does ~1000× more work), so the per-run batch — and cost — drops back down; we re-measure the knee per config.

This doesn't change Stages 0–2 (prodigy NaN fix, compile/dispatch, finishing the L3-TC conversions are correctness work, scale-independent) — those proceed now. It only sets the target for **Stage 3**.

So the one call I need from you: the benchmark scale. **My pick is d=2048.** Options: keep-tiny-and-document-the-physics / d=1024 (cheaper) / **d=2048 (recommended)** / d=4096 (max, priciest). Which way do you want to go — and should I keep Stages 0–2 moving on the toy models while you decide?

---

# Builder-Ready Implementation Spec: In-Kernel SAM 2nd-Pass (LookSAM / SG11 / SG15)

## 0. Orientation &amp; key facts established by the read

- The shipped TC megakernel is `fused_decoder_megakernel_tc&lt;Opt&gt;` in `csrc/fused/sm_90/fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:382-720`. Phase map: **P0** zero (`:427-430`) → **B0** (`:430`) → **P1** fwd+bwd tile loop (`:435-447`) → **B1** (`:449`) → **P2** grad-assembly (`:455-482`) → **B2** `sync_reset(ctx.g_next_task)` (`:483`) → opt-guarded **P2.5** GrokAdamW (`:497-536`), **P2.6** Prodigy (`:557-592`), **P2.7** Muon (`:609-663`) → **P3** apply tail (`:665-719`).
- The 2nd pass goes **between B2 (`:483`) and P2.5 (`:497`)**. This is the slot right after the reduced first-pass grad lands in `grad[total]` and after `ctx.g_next_task` has been reset to 0 by B2 — i.e. the task queue is fresh.
- `apply_optimizer&lt;LookSAM&gt;` reads `st.sam_dir` (`opt_components.cuh:341-345`); `apply_optimizer&lt;SuperGrok11/15&gt;` read `st.mu` + sharpness *indirectly* — the **mu precompute** (INTEGRATION-OPTSTAGES §4/§5) needs a `sharpness` buffer that **`FusedOptState` does not currently carry** (the ABI gap flagged at INTEGRATION-OPTSTAGES:244-268). This spec closes that gap append-only.
- GridBarrier (`megakernel_common.cuh:147-255`): sense-reversing generation counter; `sync()` = barrier only; `sync_reset(int* c)` = barrier + last-arriver zeroes `*c` before the generation bump. **Every CTA must call the same number of barriers in the same order** — divergence hangs the grid. This is risk #1.
- DecActs (`model_stage_decoder_tc.cuh:180-218`) is a **bf16 HBM activation arena carved at the FRONT of the workspace** (`acts_f` floats). It is **both** the inter-stage operand store **and** the dW operand store the P2 assembly reads. The 2nd forward **overwrites it in place** — handled in §3.

---

## 1. `fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh` — the new SAM phase

### 1a. Workspace carving (carve LAST so green cells stay byte-identical)

Edit the sizing helper `dec_tc_workspace_floats` (`fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:372-380`). Add a new term **after** the Muon term (so every existing region offset is unchanged):

```cpp
// SAM 2nd-pass scratch (LookSAM/SG11/SG15): p_backup[total] + grad_sam[total].
// Carved LAST → the 6 already-green cells (adamw/lion/grokfast/grokadamw/
// neuralgrok/prodigy/muon) keep every prior region byte-identical. Unused (and
// never allocated-large enough to matter) for non-SAM opts, but carved
// UNCONDITIONALLY so the opt-agnostic launcher (dec_tc_launcher_scratch) fits
// every OptId with ONE workspace size.
__host__ __device__ __forceinline__ int64_t dec_tc_sam_floats() {
    return (int64_t)2 * kDecTotalElems;   // [p_backup | grad_sam]
}
```
and append `+ dec_tc_sam_floats()` as the final term of `dec_tc_workspace_floats`. (Mirror the exact comment discipline of the Muon term at `:366-371`.)

In the kernel body, carve the two pointers **after** `muon_base` (`:418`), matching term order:
```cpp
// SAM 2nd-pass scratch, carved LAST (matches dec_tc_workspace_floats term order).
// muon_base's region is kDecMuon scratch; p_backup/grad_sam follow it.
float* sam_pbackup = muon_base + (dec_tc_muon_floats(nCTA));  // [total]
float* sam_gradsam = sam_pbackup + total;                     // [total]
```
&gt; `muon_base`'s allocated extent is exactly `dec_tc_muon_floats(nCTA)` floats (see the layout comment at `:415-418`), so `sam_pbackup` starts immediately after it. This is unconditional; for non-SAM opts these two pointers are simply never dereferenced.

### 1b. The new phase block (insert at `:496`, immediately before P2.5)

```cpp
    // ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    //  SAM 2ND PASS (LookSAM / SG11 / SG15 ONLY). Between B2 and P2.5.
    //  Produces st.sam_dir (LookSAM) or st.sharpness (SG11/SG15) by a GENUINE
    //  2nd fwd+bwd at perturbed weights w' = w + rho * g/||g||.
    //  Gated by host `do_sam`: on a non-SAM step the persisted buffer is reused
    //  (eager cached-direction semantics) and NO barriers are added → byte-
    //  identical to a step that skips this block. The if-constexpr restricts the
    //  CODE to these 3 opts; the runtime `do_sam` restricts WHEN it fires.
    //  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    //  BARRIER ACCOUNTING (risk #1): every CTA executes EXACTLY this sequence on
    //  a do_sam step (and NONE of it on a non-do_sam step). All CTAs share the
    //  same `do_sam` (a host scalar in FusedOptState), so the per-CTA barrier
    //  count never diverges → the sense-reversing generation stays matched.
    //    B_sam_norm  (sync,  B2.5-style 2-barrier Σg² reduce → ||g||)
    //    B_sam_pert  (sync_reset(g_next_task): publishes w', resets queue for P1')
    //    B1'         (sync:  all tiles' acts+lnvec written into grad_sam regions)
    //    B2'         (sync_reset(g_next_task): grad_sam assembled; reset for P2.x/P3)
    //    B_sam_restore (sync_reset(g_next_task): w restored; reset for P2.x/P3)
    if constexpr (Opt == OptId::LookSAM || Opt == OptId::SuperGrok11 ||
                  Opt == OptId::SuperGrok15) {
      if (st.do_sam != 0.0f) {
        // (0) GLOBAL ||g|| over the reduced first-pass grad. REUSE the P2.5
        //     machinery verbatim (deterministic ascending-CTA Σg²; loss_part /
        //     loss_out are free scratch — the reduced loss already lives in
        //     *tok.loss_out). Two barriers, exactly like P2.5a/b.
        float* sq_part = loss_part;          // [nCTA] per-CTA Σ g²
        float* norm_bc = loss_out;           // [1] broadcast ||g||
        {
            const int64_t base = total / nCTA, rem = total % nCTA;
            const int64_t e0 = (int64_t)cta * base + (cta &lt; rem ? cta : rem);
            const int64_t ecnt = base + (cta &lt; rem ? 1 : 0);
            float tsum = 0.0f;
            for (int64_t i = threadIdx.x; i &lt; ecnt; i += blockDim.x) {
                const float gv = grad[e0 + i]; tsum += gv * gv;
            }
            float* red = sm.red;
            red[threadIdx.x] = tsum; __syncthreads();
            for (int s = blockDim.x &gt;&gt; 1; s &gt; 0; s &gt;&gt;= 1) {
                if (threadIdx.x &lt; s) red[threadIdx.x] += red[threadIdx.x + s];
                __syncthreads();
            }
            if (threadIdx.x == 0) sq_part[cta] = red[0];
            bar.sync();                       // B_sam_norm_a
            if (cta == 0 &amp;&amp; threadIdx.x == 0) {
                double ss = 0.0;
                for (int c = 0; c &lt; nCTA; ++c) ss += (double)sq_part[c];
                *norm_bc = sqrtf((float)ss);
            }
            bar.sync();                       // B_sam_norm_b
        }
        const float gnorm = *norm_bc;
        const float pscale = st.rho / (gnorm + 1e-12f);   // looksam.h scale arg

        // (1) PERTURB w' = w + pscale*g into params, saving w into p_backup.
        //     looksam.h::looksam_perturb_step is THE canonical fn. Each element
        //     is owned once (flat stride over total) → no atomics, no barrier
        //     internal to the loop; one barrier AFTER so w' is visible grid-wide
        //     before the 2nd forward reads any weight tile cross-CTA. We ALSO
        //     reset the task queue here for P1' tile claiming if P1' uses it
        //     (the decoder P1 grid-strides ti=cta; it does NOT use the queue, so
        //     sync_reset's reset is harmless/defensive — keep it for symmetry
        //     with P2' which DOES drain the queue).
        for (int64_t i = (int64_t)cta * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
             i &lt; total; i += (int64_t)nCTA * blockDim.x)
            algo::looksam_perturb_step&lt;float,float&gt;(params, sam_pbackup, grad,
                                                    pscale, i);
        bar.sync_reset(ctx.g_next_task);      // B_sam_pert: w' visible; queue reset

        // (2) SECOND FULL fwd+bwd at w' → grad_sam. IDENTICAL device fns to P1+P2
        //     but writing into sam_gradsam instead of grad. DecActs is overwritten
        //     IN PLACE (recompute-in-place; see §3). NO loss accumulation needed
        //     (sam pass discards NLL). Re-bind acts/scratch exactly as the first
        //     pass; they are reused.
        //   P1' (mirror :435-447, no nll bookkeeping):
        for (int ti = cta; ti &lt; n_tiles; ti += nCTA) {
            const int g0 = ti * nrows_tile;
            const int nrows = (T - g0) &lt; nrows_tile ? (T - g0) : nrows_tile;
            (void)dectc::dectc_forward_tile(w, g0, nrows, acts, sc,
                                            tok.tokens, tok.targets,
                                            sm.sA, sm.sB, sm.red);
            dectc::dectc_backward_tile(w, g0, nrows, B, acts, sc, tok.targets,
                                       my_lnvec, sc.work2, sm.sA, sm.sB, sm.red);
            __syncthreads();
        }
        bar.sync();                           // B1': all acts (X+dY)+lnvec done
        //   P2' grad-assembly into sam_gradsam (mirror :455-475 EXACTLY, but the
        //   grad-output arg is sam_gradsam). Re-zero my_lnvec FIRST? NO — the bwd
        //   ACCUMULATES into my_lnvec with +=, and the first pass already consumed
        //   it in P2's dectc_lnvec_reduce. We MUST re-zero my_lnvec before P1'
        //   (see §3 ordering note). Build specs (specs read acts → now the 2nd
        //   pass's acts, correct), run dW, biases, embed-scan, lnvec-reduce → sam.
        if (threadIdx.x == 0) dectc::dectc_build_dw_specs(acts, B, T, sm.spec);
        __syncthreads();
        {
            dectc::DecDwSpec* spec2 = sm.spec;
            const int n_dw2 = dectc::dectc_dw_total_tiles&lt;SG_TUNED_TILE_N&gt;(spec2);
            if (kDwG &gt; 1) {
                for (int item = cta; item &lt; n_dw2 * kDwG; item += nCTA) {
                    const int gt = item / kDwG, kc = item % kDwG;
                    dectc::dectc_dw_run_tile_splitk&lt;SG_TUNED_TILE_N&gt;(
                        spec2, gt, kc, kDwG, dw_part, sm.sA, sm.sB);
                }
                bar.sync();                   // B_sam_dwk: split-K partials done
                dectc::dectc_dw_reduce_splitk&lt;SG_TUNED_TILE_N&gt;(
                    spec2, n_dw2, kDwG, dw_part, sam_gradsam, cta, nCTA);
            } else {
                for (int gt = cta; gt &lt; n_dw2; gt += nCTA)
                    dectc::dectc_dw_run_tile&lt;SG_TUNED_TILE_N&gt;(
                        spec2, gt, sam_gradsam, sm.sA, sm.sB);
            }
            dectc::dectc_dw_biases(spec2, sam_gradsam);
            dectc::dectc_embed_owner_scan(acts, tok.tokens, T, sam_gradsam, cta, nCTA);
            dectc::dectc_lnvec_reduce(lnvec_base, sam_gradsam, nCTA, cta);
        }
        bar.sync_reset(ctx.g_next_task);      // B2': grad_sam assembled; queue reset

        // (3) WRITE the SAM output. LookSAM: sam_dir = grad_sam - grad. SG11/15:
        //     sharpness = (grad_sam - grad)^2. Flat element stride; single owner;
        //     no barrier (each writes its own slice of a persisted buffer).
        if constexpr (Opt == OptId::LookSAM) {
            for (int64_t i = (int64_t)cta * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
                 i &lt; total; i += (int64_t)nCTA * blockDim.x)
                algo::looksam_set_direction&lt;float&gt;(
                    /*sam_dir=*/const_cast&lt;float*&gt;(st.sam_dir),
                    sam_gradsam, grad, i);
        } else {  // SG11 / SG15: sharpness = (g_sam - g)^2 into st.sharpness
            for (int64_t i = (int64_t)cta * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
                 i &lt; total; i += (int64_t)nCTA * blockDim.x) {
                const float d = sam_gradsam[i] - grad[i];
                const_cast&lt;float*&gt;(st.sharpness)[i] = d * d;
            }
        }

        // (4) RESTORE w from p_backup (looksam.h::looksam_restore_step). One
        //     barrier after so the restored weights are visible before P2.x/P3
        //     read params; this barrier also resets the queue for P2.6/P3 (which
        //     drain it). do_sam path's LAST barrier — count is fixed.
        for (int64_t i = (int64_t)cta * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
             i &lt; total; i += (int64_t)nCTA * blockDim.x)
            algo::looksam_restore_step&lt;float&gt;(params, sam_pbackup, i);
        bar.sync_reset(ctx.g_next_task);      // B_sam_restore: w restored; queue reset
      }
    }
    // ──────── existing P2.5 / P2.6 / P2.7 / P3 follow UNCHANGED ────────
```

### 1c. `my_lnvec` re-zero (the one subtle add inside the existing P0)

`dectc_backward_tile` **accumulates** LN-vector grads into `my_lnvec` with `+=` (`model_stage_decoder_tc.cuh:577`, `:970`). The first pass zeroed it in P0 (`:428`) and consumed it in P2's `dectc_lnvec_reduce`. Before **P1'** runs the 2nd backward, `my_lnvec` must be re-zeroed or it double-accumulates the 1st pass's LN grads. Add a zeroing loop **at the top of the SAM block, before the perturb** (cheap; `kLnVecElems=1280`):
```cpp
for (int i = threadIdx.x; i &lt; dectc::kLnVecElems; i += blockDim.x) my_lnvec[i] = 0.0f;
// (no barrier needed here: each CTA only reads its own my_lnvec in P1', and B1'
//  separates the writes from the cross-CTA dectc_lnvec_reduce read.)
```
&gt; Place it after the `||g||` reduce uses `sm.red` but it touches a different buffer, so ordering is free; simplest is right after `(void)norm_bc;`/before the perturb loop.

### 2a. `FusedOptState` (`opt_components.cuh:84-145`) — append at the end (after `aux_beta2`, `:144`):

This spec's job is to **produce** `st.sharpness`; consuming it in the SG11/15 tail is the §4/§5 stage that the ABI gap was blocking.

## 3. The 2nd P1/P2 re-invocation and DecActs recompute-in-place (risk #2)

**Exactly which device fns the 2nd pass re-invokes** (all from `model_stage_decoder_tc.cuh`, identical to the first pass — no new model code):
- **P1':** `dectc::dectc_forward_tile` (`:672`) + `dectc::dectc_backward_tile` (`:910`), per tile, same args except they now run at the perturbed `params` (bound once at `:424` as `DecWeights w = dec_bind(params)` — `w` holds *pointers* into `params`, so after the in-place perturb it automatically reads w').
- **P2':** `dectc_build_dw_specs` (`:1086`), `dectc_dw_total_tiles` (`:1111`), `dectc_dw_run_tile` / `dectc_dw_run_tile_splitk`+`dectc_dw_reduce_splitk` (`:1121`/`:1210`/`:1250`), `dectc_dw_biases` (`:1275`), `dectc_embed_owner_scan` (`:1295`), `dectc_lnvec_reduce` (`:1326`) — **with `sam_gradsam` as the grad-output arg** instead of `grad`.

**DecActs recompute-in-place correctness.** `acts` (the bf16 activation arena, `acts_f` floats at the front of the workspace, bound at `:420`) is the *same* buffer for both passes. The ordering that makes overwriting it safe:

1. The **first pass's** P2 (`:455-475`) reads `acts` (via `dectc_build_dw_specs` → `spec.dY/X` point into `acts`) and writes the reduced first-pass grad into `grad`. After **B2** (`:483`), the first pass is **completely done consuming `acts`**.
2. The SAM block runs **after B2**, so when P1' overwrites `acts.X_in[...]/dY_*` for the 2nd forward, no reader of the first-pass acts is still live. **B1'** then fences all 2nd-pass acts writes before P2' reads them cross-CTA (the dW owner CTA for a tile reads `acts` rows produced by *other* CTAs).
3. Per-CTA `sc` (`DecTileScratch`, bound at `:421`) is single-buffered and reused tile-by-tile *within* a pass — the first pass already finished all tiles by B1, so the 2nd pass reusing it is fine (same lifetime contract the first pass relies on).
4. **`grad` is never overwritten by the SAM pass** — step (3) reads `grad` (first-pass) and `sam_gradsam` (2nd-pass) to produce `sam_dir`/`sharpness`. P2.5/P2.6/P3 then consume the unchanged first-pass `grad`. This is essential: LookSAM's apply (`looksam.h:79-91`) and SG's apply both use the *original* grad `g`, blended with the SAM-derived direction.

&gt; The keystone invariant: **P2.x and P3 must see the original `grad` and the restored `params`.** The SAM block touches `params` (perturb→restore, netting to identity by `looksam_restore_step`) and `sam_gradsam`/`sam_pbackup` (scratch) and `st.sam_dir`/`st.sharpness` (persisted outputs) — never `grad`.

---

## 4. Barrier correctness (risk #1) — the rigorous argument

The GridBarrier is sense-reversing (`megakernel_common.cuh:157-210`): each `sync()`/`sync_reset()` samples `g_generation`, fences, `atomicAdd(g_arrived,1)`; the last arriver resets `g_arrived=0` (and, for `sync_reset`, zeroes the passed counter) then bumps `g_generation`; non-last CTAs spin until the generation advances. **Correctness requires every CTA to execute the identical *sequence* of barrier calls** — if CTA A calls N barriers and CTA B calls N+1, B's extra `atomicAdd` lands in a set that A never completes, and the grid hangs (or worse, laps).

The added barriers are **gated by `st.do_sam`, a single host scalar identical across all CTAs**, and by `if constexpr (Opt ∈ {LookSAM,SG11,SG15})`, a compile-time constant. Therefore:
- On a **non-SAM step** (`do_sam==0`) or a **non-SAM optimizer**: zero added barriers → the megakernel's barrier sequence is exactly today's (B0,B1,B2,[P2.x],…). Byte-identical.
- On a **SAM step**: every CTA adds **exactly the same** barriers in the same order:
  `B_sam_norm_a (sync)`, `B_sam_norm_b (sync)`, `B_sam_pert (sync_reset)`, `B1' (sync)`, `[B_sam_dwk (sync) iff kDwG&gt;1]`, `B2' (sync_reset)`, `B_sam_restore (sync_reset)`. The `kDwG&gt;1` branch is a **compile-time constant** (`kDwG = dectc::kDecDwSplitK`, `:408`) — uniform across CTAs — so even that conditional barrier is grid-uniform. No data-dependent barrier anywhere in the block.

**Generation arithmetic:** every added barrier is a full `sync`/`sync_reset` (matched arrive+release), so the generation counter advances once per added barrier for all CTAs in lockstep; the sense never desynchronizes. **Queue-reset placement:** the three `sync_reset(ctx.g_next_task)` calls (B_sam_pert, B2', B_sam_restore) zero `g_next_task` so the next queue-draining phase starts at 0. The downstream consumers that drain the queue are P2.6 (Prodigy `:567`) and P3 (`:671`) — but those are the GrokAdamW/Prodigy/Muon/AdamW tails, and **SG11/15/LookSAM use neither Prodigy's P2.6 nor a queue in P3's SG/LookSAM branch** (the SG/LookSAM P3 still iterates the queue at `:671`). So the **final** SAM barrier (B_sam_restore) leaving `g_next_task==0` is exactly what P3's `next_block()` needs. Correct.

&gt; One must-verify for the builder: P1' for the decoder uses `for (ti=cta; ti&lt;n_tiles; ti+=nCTA)` (grid-stride, **no task queue**), identical to the first P1 (`:438`). So B_sam_pert's queue reset is defensive only for P1'; it is *load-bearing* for P2' (the dW split-K and lnvec reduce iterate `cta`-strided, also no queue — but `dectc_dw_*` with `kDwG&gt;1` uses `bar.sync()` only, no queue). Net: the queue is actually only consumed by P2.6/P3; resetting at B2' and B_sam_restore guarantees a clean counter for them. Keeping all three `sync_reset` is the safe, uniform choice.

```cpp
    if (optimizer == "looksam")     return 4;   // OptId::LookSAM (in-kernel SAM 2nd pass)
    if (optimizer == "supergrok11") return 8;   // OptId::SuperGrok11
```

### 5b. The decoder TC launcher `mega_decoder_real_adamw_tc` (`mega_decoder_real_adamw_tc_launcher.cu:177-214`)
Add three `switch` cases that dispatch the new instantiations, and bind `st.sharpness` / `st.sam_dir`:
```cpp
        case OptId::LookSAM:
            // st.sam_dir bound from the persisted SAM-direction buffer (see below).

                ctx, params, tok, grad, lr, step, st, stream, nCTA);
```

- Host sizing in `dispatch.cpp:732`: bump `min_state` for these opts (LookSAM: `3*total+1+total`; SG11/15: `3*total+1+total` for sharpness, plus the mu/phi pack — mirror the Prodigy `4*total+4` pattern and the SG `extra`-slice packing the surrogate cells already use).

The vit/mamba TC megakernels are **structurally identical** (confirmed: `fused_vit_megakernel.cuh` has the same P0/B0→P1/B1→P2/B2(`sync_reset`)→P2.5/P2.6/P2.7→P3 layout, `:178/:204/:233/:458/:565/:616/:659`; same `vit_tc_workspace_floats` carve-last discipline `:381-389`; same Muon/Prodigy scratch terms). The delta is a near-mechanical port:

- **ViT** (`fused_vit_megakernel.cuh`):
  - Add `vit_tc_sam_floats()` = `2*kVitTotalElems`, append to `vit_tc_workspace_floats` (`:381`) as the last term.
  - Carve `sam_pbackup`/`sam_gradsam` after `muon_base` (the vit body's muon carve, analogous to decoder `:418`).
  - Insert the **same** `if constexpr (LookSAM||SG11||SG15) { if (do_sam) {...} }` block between B2 (`:549`) and P2.5 (`:565`). The 2nd fwd+bwd calls the **vit** tile fns (`vittc::vit_forward_tile`/`vit_backward_tile` — the vit analogues of `dectc_forward_tile`/`dectc_backward_tile`) and the vit P2 assembly fns, writing into `sam_gradsam`. ViT's input is **float patches** (`ViTInputCtx`), not int tokens — the SAM block doesn't touch input handling, so this is transparent.
  - Re-zero the vit per-CTA `my_lnvec` before P1' (same `+=`-accumulation hazard).
  - `vit_tc_launcher`/`mega_vit_real_adamw_tc_launcher.cu` switch: add the 3 cases; bind `st.sam_dir`/`st.sharpness` to the extended vit state buffer.
  - dispatch.py: add the 3 vit cells to `_FUSED_L3_REAL`/`_L3_WGMMA_CELLS`.
- **Mamba** (`fused_mamba_megakernel.cuh`):
  - Same insertion, but **note the `.audit_notes.md` caveat** (BATCH-2 / "mamba×prodigy A/A/A FAILS — scheduling-exposed race in the shared mamba forward under prodigy's register pressure", echoed at dispatch.py `:1296-1299`). The SAM 2nd pass **re-runs the mamba forward twice** and adds register pressure → it risks the **same determinism failure**. **Recommendation: land mamba×{looksam,SG11,SG15} as code-present but gate them OUT of `_L3_WGMMA_CELLS` until A/A/A determinism is confirmed** (exactly how mamba×prodigy is handled — "landed-dormant"). The decoder and vit are the supported lanes; mamba is dormant pending the determinism fix.

&gt; The `wgmma_tail_opt_id` mapping is model-independent, so 5a covers all three models; only the per-model `_L3_WGMMA_CELLS` membership and the per-model launcher switch differ.

## 7. Occupancy / smem check (cite + confirm)

The 2nd pass adds **only HBM buffers** (`p_backup`+`grad_sam`, 2·total floats ≈ 3.4 MB total; the DecActs/scratch are reused, not grown) and **register-light elementwise loops + reused device fns**. It adds **no static smem** — `DecTcSmem` (`:315-323`) is unchanged (same `sA/sB/red/spec`), and the 2nd pass reuses `sm.red`/`sm.sA`/`sm.sB`/`sm.spec`. Therefore the launch-guard at `mega_decoder_real_adamw_tc_launcher.cu`→`launch_fused_decoder_megakernel_tc` (`fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:744-749`):
```cpp
cudaOccupancyMaxActiveBlocksPerMultiprocessor(&amp;occ, ..., /*dynamicSMemBytes=*/0);
if (occ &lt; 1) return cudaErrorLaunchOutOfResources;
```
**still passes** — the occupancy is governed by static smem (unchanged) + register count. The SAM block's registers are released between phases (the elementwise loops are small and the heavy fns are the *same* `dectc_*` already accounted for in the first pass's occupancy). The one-CTA/SM ≥1 hang-freedom guard (`:749`) is unaffected. The vit guard is the twin at `fused_vit_megakernel.cuh:800-804`. **Confirm post-build** that `occ&gt;=1` still holds (register pressure from the added `if constexpr` body could in principle bump max-reg; the body is small, so this is expected to hold, but it is the one occupancy line to watch in the SASS).

## 8. Parity gate (the keystone)

Add `test_tc_sam_2nd_pass_parity` to `tests/hw/test_decoder_tc.py`, modeled on `test_tc_single_step_grad_parity` (`:497-562`) and reusing `decoder_oracle.py` + `_bf16_faithful_oracle` (`:349`).

**Oracle (fp64/eager, the two-grad keystone):**
1. Build the eager decoder + named params (`_eager_decoder("cpu", seed=…)`, `:512`).
2. First backward at `w` → `g` per tensor (the existing `decoder_backward(W64, c64)`, `:522`).
3. Compute `‖g‖ = sqrt(Σ_tensors Σ g²)` (global, fp64).
4. Perturb `w' = w + rho·g/‖g‖` per tensor (`looksam.h` math, fp64).
5. **Recompute grad at w'** — a genuine 2nd `decoder_forward`+`decoder_backward(W64', …)` → `g_sam`.
6. Restore `w`.
7. Oracle output: **LookSAM** `sam_dir = g_sam − g`; **SG11/15** `sharpness = (g_sam − g)²`, per tensor.

**Kernel side:** run the TC step with `do_sam=1`, `rho` matching the oracle, read back the persisted `st.sam_dir`/`st.sharpness` buffer (expose it through the state buffer the same way `grad` is exposed for the existing grad parity, `dispatch.cpp:739-749`).

**Tolerance:** the keystone is the **two-grad difference**. The SG-family per-element tol is **~3e-5** (`.audit_notes.md` PARITY ORACLE: "SG-family ~3e-5, fp32 storage"; a real bug diverges 1e-1…1e+3 → 6+ orders of margin). Use **fp32 storage** for the parity (bf16 ≈ 1e-3 masks bugs — `.audit_notes.md` "use fp32"). Concretely:
- Compare `sam_dir`/`sharpness` against the fp64 oracle at **rel ≤ 3e-5** for the difference buffer (consistent with the SG-family oracle tol). The existing per-tensor structural witness (layer-0 ≈ layer-1, `:546-555`) carries over — a broken 2nd backward makes early layers' `sam_dir` ≫ late.
- Keep a **bf16-faithful floor** print (the 2nd grad rides the same bf16 noise as the first, `_TC_GRAD_REL_WEIGHTS=0.15` for the raw `g_sam`); but the *difference* `g_sam−g` cancels common-mode bf16 noise, so the tight 3e-5 gate is on the *difference*, calibrated against `(oracle_diff − fp64_diff)`.
- **Cadence faithfulness sub-test:** run 2·k steps with `do_sam=1` only on `step%k==0`; assert the kernel's `sam_dir` is **byte-identical** across the k−1 intervening steps (the persisted buffer is reused, matching `looksam.py:285-289` cached-direction semantics) and updates only on SAM steps.

---

## Top-3 risks &amp; concrete mitigations

1. **Barrier sense-reversal / generation correctness across the added B1'/B2' rendezvous (#1 risk).**
   *Mitigation:* gate the *entire* added sequence on `st.do_sam` (a single host scalar, identical across all CTAs) **and** `if constexpr (Opt∈{LookSAM,SG11,SG15})` (compile-time) — so the per-CTA barrier *count* never diverges. The only intra-block conditional barrier (`kDwG&gt;1` split-K) keys off a **compile-time constant** (`dectc::kDecDwSplitK`), not data, so it too is grid-uniform. Every added rendezvous is a full `sync`/`sync_reset` (matched arrive+release) advancing the generation once for all CTAs in lockstep. The three `sync_reset(ctx.g_next_task)` leave the queue counter at 0 for the downstream queue-draining phases (P2.6/P3). **Verify** by asserting in the parity test that a `do_sam=0` step is byte-identical to today's path (no extra barriers ⇒ no behavior change), and that a `do_sam=1` step does not hang under `cuda-memcheck --tool synccheck`.

2. **DecActs recompute-in-place — the 2nd forward overwriting the bf16 acts the 1st pass's P2 already consumed (#2 risk).**
   *Mitigation:* the SAM block runs strictly **after B2**, by which point the first pass has fully consumed `acts` (its P2 dW/embed/lnvec assembly completed before B2's release). The 2nd forward overwrites `acts` in place; **B1'** fences all 2nd-pass acts writes before P2' reads them cross-CTA. Critically, **`grad` is never overwritten** — the SAM pass writes a *separate* `sam_gradsam`, and `params` perturb→restore nets to identity, so P2.5/P2.6/P3 see the original `grad` and restored `w`. **Plus** re-zero the per-CTA `my_lnvec` before P1' (the backward `+=`-accumulates LN grads; the 1st pass left it consumed-but-nonzero). **Verify** with the per-tensor structural witness (layer-0 ≈ layer-1 in `sam_dir`) — a stale-acts bug makes early layers' SAM direction diverge ≫ late.

3. **Cadence faithfulness — the in-kernel `do_sam` must reproduce eager's every-k-steps cached-direction semantics exactly (#3 risk).**
   *Mitigation:* the host sets `do_sam = (step % sam_k == 0)` mirroring `looksam.should_sam_step()` (`looksam.py:296-299`), and on `do_sam==0` the kernel **skips the entire SAM block** and the apply tail reuses the **persisted** `st.sam_dir`/`st.sharpness` from the most recent SAM step — byte-identical to eager reusing `state["sam_direction"]` on intervening steps (`looksam.py:272-289`). The persisted buffer lives in the optimizer **state** tensor (not transient workspace) so it survives across launches. **Verify** with the cadence sub-test (§8): over 2·k steps, assert `sam_dir` is bit-stable across intervening steps and updates only on SAM steps, matching the eager reference's cadence.

**Files a builder touches (all read-only-verified here):**
`csrc/fused/sm_90/fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh` (sizing helper `:372`, kernel carve `:418`, new SAM block `:496`, `my_lnvec` re-zero), `csrc/fused/sm_90/opt_components.cuh` (`FusedOptState` `:144`, `FusedScalars` `:213`, `apply_scalars` `:246`, `rebase_state` `:121`), `csrc/fused/sm_90/mega_decoder_real_adamw_tc_launcher.cu` (switch `:177`, state bindings `:151`), `csrc/bindings/dispatch.cpp` (`wgmma_tail_opt_id` `:583`, mirror `FusedScalars` `:204`, `fused_step` sig `:607`+scalar POD `:768`, `min_state` `:732`), `grokking_optimizers/dispatch.py` (`_FUSED_L3_REAL` `:667`, `_L3_WGMMA_CELLS` `:1224`, `fused_train_step` cadence `:1350`), `tests/hw/test_decoder_tc.py` (new parity + cadence tests). Understood — the roofline optimization is a **loop until convergence**, not a one-shot pass. For each cell: propose an optimization → incremental rebuild → measure the roofline % → keep it if the % improves (and parity stays green), revert it if not → repeat until a run of attempts stops improving it, meaning that cell has hit its ceiling. # H100 L3-TC Optimizer Audit — Findings (READ-ONLY)

## Bottom line for the owner

**The cells are NOT secretly homogenized to AdamW at the kernel level.** On decoder and ViT, every optimizer's distinctive computation is genuinely present, wired by `if constexpr (Opt == OptId::X)`, and calls its own canonical `csrc/algorithms/&lt;opt&gt;.h` function. I traced all 10 and confirmed distinct math, not a shared AdamW tail. The single-source guard has real teeth, and the HW gate is genuinely non-vacuous (forces the distinctive feature ON, with liveness asserts and a tight state check that a swapped-in AdamW would fail).

**BUT three real corners were cut, and the owner's instinct is partly right:**

1. **SG11/SG15 DO run as ≈AdamW in the actual race** — not because the kernel is wrong, but because the meta-net is never trained on the L3 path (rescale stays at its init value 0 → mu≈0). The kernel faithfully computes `mu=rescale·phi` *if given trained weights*, but on L3 the weights never get trained. **The code documents this candidly** (`grokking_race_v2.py:1260-1262`, `:1349-1351`): *"without bilevel training the meta-net stays at init (rescale=0 ⇒ mu≈0), so the L3 SG11 race is ≈AdamW."* This is a disclosed design/fidelity gap, not hidden homogenization.

2. **The gate's "load-bearing" multistep parity checks for grokadamw and prodigy DO NOT EXIST** (vaporware). The single-step gate openly admits it is BLIND to grokadamw's grad-norm clip (inert at step 1) and prodigy's d-adaptation (d=d0 at step 1), and points to `_multistep_grokadamw_parity()` / `_prodigy_multistep_parity()` as the real check — but those functions are referenced only in comments and are never defined or called (`tests/hw/test_l3tc_tail_gate.py:304,313,321,332,339`; only `run_cell_gate` is wired to pytest at :1062). So the distinctive feature of those two is not gate-verified on the L3-TC kernel.

3. **The non-vacuous HW gate never runs in CI.** It is `@pytest.mark.hw` (`:1060`); every CI runner is `ubuntu-latest` and `pytest_cpu` runs `-m "not hw"`. Nothing in `ci.yml`/`compile.py --self-test`/`scripts` invokes it. The anti-rubber-stamp gate exists and is good, but its execution is manual on a Hopper box.

Plus: **on Mamba**, prodigy + looksam are code-landed but **dispatch-blocked** (dormant, env-gated behind `SG_MAMBA_*_PROBE`), and **SG11/SG15 are code-absent entirely** — all three blocked on a documented shared mamba-forward A/A/A race. So 5 of the "26 cells" (mamba × {prodigy, looksam, sg11, sg15}, plus the dispatch makes mamba SG a loud-fail) are not actually live.

## Detail on the four audit questions

**1. Distinct-math wiring — PASS (decoder/vit).** `apply_optimizer` (`opt_components.cuh:329-463`) has a genuinely distinct `if constexpr` branch per OptId, each calling its own canonical function; the header docstring's claim *"no AdamW fallback"* holds. The distinguishing precompute that FEEDS each tail (NS `orth`, meta-net `mu`, `sam_dir`, prodigy `d`, sharpness) is really computed in `fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh` / `fused_vit_megakernel.cuh` P2.4/P2.45/P2.6/P2.7 — I verified the muon NS matmul loop, the SG meta-net phi forward, and the looksam/SG real second forward+backward (`:606-609`). Every canonical header is genuinely distinct math (lion sign, grokfast/grokadamw EMA amplify, prodigy d·g, neuralgrok psi, looksam sam_dir blend, muon decay·p+scale·orth, SG smart_grad). The shared AdamW *apply tail* line they all contain is correct — it runs on each optimizer's *already-transformed* effective gradient, not raw g.

**2. Single-source integrity — PASS with one soft spot.** Manifest exists with all 11 canonical hashes + 10 binding-region hashes (`scripts/optimizer_math_manifest.json`), run in CI (`ci.yml:1211`). The structural `#include` check and content-hash drift detection are hard-fails; re-inlined Adam math is regex-detected and fails. **Soft spot:** the apply-symbol-call assertion (proving the canonical step is actually *called*, not just included) was downgraded to a **WARN, not fail** (`check_math_single_source.py:375-384`), and SG2's check is warn-only — so an include-without-call could pass. Not bypassed, but weaker than the comments imply.

**3. Gate non-vacuousness — MOSTLY PASS; two gaps.** The gate is well-designed: it drives the REAL eager optimizer with the kernel's grad injected; the (1b) STATE check (tol 1e-4) is explicitly "THE DECISIVE CHECK" because step-1 params collapse to sign(g) and can't distinguish optimizers (`:924-932`). For SG/looksam it forces the distinctive feature ON and has liveness asserts that fail on a skipped phase. **Gap A:** grokadamw-clip and prodigy-d are admittedly unexercised at step 1, and the promised multistep backstops are vaporware. **Gap B:** the entire gate is HW-only and runs in no CI job.

**4. L3-vs-eager fidelity — the key asymmetry.** "Kernel faithfully runs the meta-net IF given trained weights" vs "weights never get trained on this path":
- **NeuralGrok**: weights DO get trained on L3 (host amplifier `train_amplifier_step` runs each step, psi_pack re-extracted pre-launch) → fully functional.
- **GrokAdamW**: host forward computes train_loss/grok-signal pre-launch so adaptive α binds → functional.
- **SG11/SG15**: weights do NOT get trained on L3 (`meta_step` skipped — needs an autograd graph the megakernel doesn't return) → meta-net frozen at rescale=0 → **≈AdamW in production**. Kernel is faithful; the training is the missing piece. Self-documented in `grokking_race_v2.py` and `.audit_notes.md`.

### ASSISTANT [2026-06-11T20:16:58]
Good question — and it's worth being precise, because "non-deterministic" here means something specific and bad, not the benign run-to-run wobble people often shrug at in ML.

**What it means concretely:** run the *exact same* training step — identical weights, identical input batch, identical everything — twice, and you get *different* numbers out. Not "converges to a slightly different model after 1000 steps," but the very first step's gradients/loss differ run-to-run. The project's gate calls this **A/A/A**: run input A three times, demand bit-identical results. The mamba forward, under the double-forward path, fails it.

**Why a GPU kernel does this** — it's a parallel-ordering problem, and it comes down to two usual culprits:

1. **Race in a reduction.** The kernel sums contributions (gradients, norms) across thousands of threads/CTAs running concurrently. If the *order* they add into a shared accumulator depends on which thread happens to finish first, the result varies — because floating-point addition isn't associative. `(a + b) + c` and `a + (b + c)` give *different* rounding in float. So a timing-dependent summation order → different rounding → different answer each run. The prodigy fix found exactly this: a "work-steal" queue where CTAs grabbed work items in whatever order they raced to claim them. The fix was a **fixed-partition** reduction — every CTA owns a predetermined slice and sums in a fixed ascending order, so the order is identical every run.

2. **Reading uninitialized or out-of-bounds memory.** If a thread reads a scratch slot before it's written (or past the valid region), it picks up whatever garbage was there — which depends on what ran before. Prodigy had this too: a ragged-tile GEMM read past the valid rows into the workspace tail where the d-adaptation values sat, which is what actually produced the NaN.

**Why it's a real bug, not noise:** if a kernel is non-deterministic, you can't trust its parity check — it might match the fp64 oracle on one run and diverge on the next, so "it passed" means nothing. Worse, a race can silently corrupt training or, as prodigy showed, blow up to NaN. That's why the project treats A/A/A bit-identity as a hard gate and why I won't let a cell ship until it's deterministic — a non-deterministic cell is an unfixed correctness defect wearing a passing-parity disguise.

**Why it blocks specifically the mamba SAM cells:** looksam/SG11/SG15 run the mamba forward *a second time* at perturbed weights (for the sharpness/SAM gradient). Something on that double-forward path under register pressure exposes a race that the single-forward cells (adamw, muon, etc.) don't trigger — which is why muon/mamba ships fine but those four were landed dormant rather than shipped broken. The agent running now is hunting that exact race so they can be un-dormanted honestly.

- The **full SG2 meta-net as a standalone persistent megakernel**: `sg2_meta_stages&lt;Dims&gt;` (S0..S5 CSA/HCA/GRU/PEER/apply), `sg2_meta_optimizer_megakernel`, and `launch_sg2_meta_optimizer_tail` — all in `csrc/fused/sm_90/opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh` (the whole file, 1202 lines). Math is called verbatim from canonical `csrc/algorithms/supergrok2.h`.
- The host binding **`sg2_meta_optimizer_tail` is ALREADY registered** in pybind (bindings.cpp:3599, body 1878-…; `sg2_ws_stride` at bindings.cpp:3630/1898). So the optimizer-only path is wired end to end.
- **Part (A) parity is GREEN with no GPU**: the fp64 structural mirror vs a clean `csa_hca_step_one` oracle agrees to ~1e-16 across N∈{5,17,64,200} + a 200-step trajectory (tests/hw/test_sg2_megakernel.py:96-135, tol 1e-11 / 1e-10).
- The **in-kernel SAM 2nd-pass** (perturb → 2nd fwd+bwd → `sharpness=(g_sam−g)²`) is live for SG11/SG15 in the decoder/vit megakernels (P2.4, fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:511-651; .sam_spec.md). SG2 reuses it unchanged.
- `OptId::SuperGrok2 = 10` **already exists** (opt_components.cuh:56). The current `apply_optimizer&lt;SuperGrok2&gt;` is a plain Adam apply on a precomputed `st.smart_grad` (opt_components.cuh:454-462) — i.e. the L1 tail, NOT the meta-net.

**Missing (this task's four deliverables):**
1. **In-kernel segmented sort** — the only NEW device-code piece of size. Eliminates the one retained `torch.argsort` precompute.
2. **Compose the meta-net into the model megakernel** as the optimizer phase (today it's a separate launcher with its own grid barrier).
3. **Wire sharpness** from the SAM 2nd-pass into the meta-net's input_proj.
4. **Routing**: `wgmma_tail_opt_id` → real OptId, dispatch.py cell sets, launcher cases.

**HONEST SCOPE.** This is multi-step and the hardest cell. "L3-TC + single-step parity vs the per-op oracle" is achievable. **"Groks" is NOT achievable in this task** — blocked by an independent CSA/HCA lightning-indexer fidelity gap (§7). Distinguish the two at every gate.

---

## 1. The segmented sort (largest new device-code piece)

### 1a. What the reference does, and the tie-semantics trap

The per-op oracle sorts each tensor's flat grad vector by `|grad|` **ascending** with a plain `torch::sort`:

&gt; `auto sorted = sort_keys.sort(/*dim=*/0, /*descending=*/false);` — supergrok2_sm90.cuh:1642 (context :1630-1644 explains WHY: CSA/HCA are order-DEPENDENT sequence attention; the eager net was trained on `g.abs().argsort()` ascending, supergrok2.py:676; the bilevel adjoint oracle uses the same ascending order, supergrok2_bilevel_adjoint.h:543).

The sort key is `|grad|` set in `sg2_input_proj_sort` (supergrok2.h:144 `sort_keys[idx]=fabsf(g)`). Only the **CSA/HCA/GRU/PEER** stages are order-dependent; the final PEER expert output is per-element (permutation-equivariant), so it is unsorted once via the same perm at apply (S5, opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:969-975).

**CRITICAL tie-break rail** (INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:392-400, opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:33-42): `torch.sort` on CUDA is **NOT stable**. The device segmented sort must reproduce **that** unstable ordering, **NOT** `argsort(stable=True)`. On tied `|grad|` an unstable-vs-stable mismatch breaks ties differently → different sorted order → CSA/HCA contexts diverge past 1e-5 over a trajectory. The megakernel and the parity oracle must consume the **same** `perm`.

&gt; **Builder warning:** reproducing torch's *exact* unstable tie order bit-for-bit in a hand-written bitonic kernel is hard — torch's CUDA sort tie order is an implementation detail (radix/segmented-sort internals), not a spec. There are two viable builder strategies, pick explicitly and document:
&gt; - **(A) Tie-break by index in the device sort** (comparator: `(|g_a|, a) &lt; (|g_b|, b)`), and **change the oracle's precompute to match** (the Python driver in INTEGRATION-NOTES.md §2 step 2 / the part-B test would build `perm` with the *same* deterministic index tie-break, e.g. via a key that folds the index). This makes both sides deterministic and equal — the cleanest A/A/A story — but it is a *parity definition change*, so the per-op oracle's `csa_hca_step_one` sort at supergrok2_sm90.cuh:1642 must be kept in lock-step (gate it).
&gt; - **(B) Keep the one `torch.argsort` precompute** (the current standalone path) and do NOT build the device sort. This trivially matches the oracle's ties (same torch call) and still gives "1 argsort + 1 persistent kernel" — but it does NOT achieve "zero extra launches", which is the whole point of folding the sort in. INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:382-390 calls the device sort the explicit path-to-zero.
&gt;
&gt; Recommendation: ship **(A)** for the maximal-elimination goal, with the comparator tie-break made identical on both the device sort and the oracle's perm builder, and add the tie-probe sub-test (§8). Tied `|grad|` is rare in fp32 random grads, so (A)'s risk is bounded and the index tie-break is fully deterministic (A/A/A-clean by construction).

### 1b. Design — per-tensor in-CTA segmented bitonic sort (STAGE −1)

The header is explicitly written so the sort drops in as **STAGE −1**: it only needs `perm`/`unsort` populated before S0; no other stage changes (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:55-57, 264, 1013-1014; INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:382-390).

- **Per-tensor, in one CTA.** Each CTA already owns whole tensors (tensor-per-CTA, opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:62-66, 1143-1150). Sort that tensor's `[N]` keys (`|grad|`) CTA-locally in its workspace before S0 — no cross-CTA coupling, no grid barrier (consistent with the existing zero-grid-barrier-between-stages design).
- **Algorithm.** In-CTA **bitonic sort** over `[N]` keys, transcribed as `__syncthreads()`-separated passes (the substrate discipline). Carry `(key, original_index)` pairs so the comparator can tie-break by index (strategy A). `N` is a single tensor's numel; the largest race param is ~1e5 (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:53). For `N` larger than fits comfortably in smem/registers, do a **key buffer in the workspace** (add two `[Nmax]` int/float slots to `sg2_ws_stride`) and bitonic over global/workspace memory with barrier-separated passes — `O(N log²N)` compares, fine for these sizes.
- **Outputs:** `perm[r]` = original row for sorted row r; `unsort[i]` = sorted position of original row i (= `perm.argsort()`, opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:274-276). Write both, indices LOCAL to `[0, N)`.
- **Workspace:** extend `sg2_ws_stride&lt;Dims&gt;` (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:425-448) and `sg2_carve_ws` (:450-475) with the key/index scratch (`+2*Nmax`). Mirror the change in `sg2_ws_stride_py` / prefer binding the C++ stride (INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:277-298). **The host `sg2_ws_stride` is bound (bindings.cpp:3630) — keep it authoritative so host carve and device carve can never drift.**

Bitonic is the right pick over radix here: simple barrier-separated passes match the megakernel discipline, it's deterministic (A/A/A by construction), and the index-carried comparator handles ties cleanly.

## 2. Composing the meta-net into the model megakernel (decoder primary)

This is the L3 step: the model fwd+bwd+reduce, then the SG2 meta-net as the optimizer phase, in **one** persistent kernel sharing **one** `PersistentContext`. The seam is documented at INTEGRATION-NOTES.md §3 (302-339).

### 2a. Decoder phase map (the insertion target)

`fused_decoder_megakernel_tc&lt;Opt&gt;` (fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:402+; phase overview :19-37):
```
P0 zero (:452-456) → B0 sync (:456) → P1 fwd+bwd (:458-475) → B1 sync (:475)
→ P2 grad-assembly/reduce (:477-509) → B2 sync_reset(g_next_task) (:509)
→ [P2.4 SAM 2nd-pass :511-651] [P2.5 GrokAdamW :654][P2.6 Prodigy :707][P2.7 Muon :791]
→ P3 apply (:884-963)
```
After P2 the **reduced first-pass grad is in the global grad buffer and the task queue is reset** (B2, :509) — exactly the precondition the meta-net needs.

### 2b. The SG2 composition (replace `apply_optimizer&lt;SuperGrok2&gt;` with the meta-net)

Guard the whole SG2 path with `if constexpr (Opt == OptId::SuperGrok2)`, mirroring SG11/15's guard shape (fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:546-547, 879, 919). Sequence inside the single kernel:

1. **P2.4 SAM 2nd-pass — reuse verbatim.** Add `OptId::SuperGrok2` to the existing P2.4 guard (fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:546-547). It already writes `st.sharpness[i]=(g_sam−g)²` (:645) and restores params, then `sync_reset(g_next_task)` (:650) leaving `g_next_task=0` and grad untouched. SG2 consumes `st.sharpness` exactly like SG11/15. (Per .sam_spec.md, `do_sam` is a uniform host scalar; non-SAM steps are byte-identical to today.)

2. **Stage the meta weight bundle into smem once** (`sg2_stage_weights&lt;Dims&gt;`, opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:367-397, 1136-1138). ~9 KB fp32 with defaults; total smem budget noted at 35.3 KB SMEM-resident (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:211-227). **Builder caution:** the decoder kernel already carries DecActs + dW + Prodigy/Muon/SAM scratch in smem/registers; check occupancy ≥ 1 at launch (the hang-freedom contract, opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:1178-1183) — SG2's bundle + the decoder's working set may pressure smem. The generated `mega_vit_supergrok2.cu:3` already reports `smem=66560/233472` for the L1/L3-surrogate cell; the composed TC cell must be re-measured.

3. **Segmented sort (STAGE −1, §1)** per tensor — between B2/P2.4 and the meta loop, recomputing `perm`/`unsort` from the freshly-reduced grad (INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:334-338: perm must be computed AFTER the model bwd). No extra launch.

4. **Inline `sg2_meta_stages&lt;Dims&gt;` per tensor** instead of `apply_optimizer&lt;Opt&gt;` in P3 (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:985-1108, INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:320-322). Drain the (reset) task queue with `q.next_block` (the same work-steal loop as :1144-1150). **Drop the standalone kernel's final `bar.sync()` (:1154)** — redundant inside the combined cell, which exits after apply like P3 (INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:320-322).

5. **Workspace** must hold BOTH the model's grad-partial workspace AND SG2's per-CTA `ws_stride` slices (INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:329-332). They're used in disjoint phases (model reduce, then SG2), so they MAY alias if sized for the max; simplest is two regions. Append `dec_tc_sg2_floats(nCTA, Nmax) = nCTA * sg2_ws_stride&lt;Dims&gt;(Nmax)` as the **FINAL** term in `dec_tc_workspace_floats` (fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:391-400) so the 6 green cells stay byte-identical (the .sam_spec.md "carve LAST" discipline, :372). Carve it after `dec_tc_looksam_floats()` (:399), exactly as SAM's `sam_backup`/`sam_grad` were carved after muon.

6. **Keep optimizer state permanently packed** (INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:323-327): the model's grad reduction already writes the flat grad; SG2 reads/writes the same packed `exp_avg/exp_avg_sq/mu/slow/gru_state` in place — no per-step pack/unpack. `gru_state` is `[total*gru_hidden]` row-major (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:271, 1012).

### 2c. State ABI for the composed cell

The decoder TC launcher (mega_decoder_real_adamw_tc_launcher.cu) carves persisted state per OptId (the Prodigy/SG11/15 pattern at :151-180). SG2 needs the largest persisted block:
- `[m | v | mu | slow | gru_state(total*GH) | loss | sharpness(total) | perm(total int) | unsort(total int) | meta_bundle]`.
- `mu` and `slow` are the two per-element EMAs `sg2_apply_step` consumes (supergrok2.h:399-400, 419-424). The current generic SG2 cell only binds `st.smart_grad=extra` (mega_vit_supergrok2.cu:42); the composed TC cell must bind the **full** SG2 state set (mu/slow/gru_state) + the meta bundle pointers.
- `sg_rescale` already rides `FusedScalars` (opt_components.cuh:198, 284, 320). SG2's per-tensor scalars (alpha/gru_decay/lamb_eff/beta1/bc1/bc2, opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:301-312) are per-tensor arrays — the launcher must build these from the live optimizer (the per-op path already computes layer-scaled betas/alphas per tensor; INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:236-242). **Builder note:** the single global `FusedScalars` cannot hold per-tensor arrays — this is the same representability constraint that blocks grokadamw's per-tensor clip from the global scalars (dispatch.py:1430-1432). SG2 needs per-tensor scalar **buffers** threaded through state/workspace, not `FusedScalars` — bump the launcher's state sizing and bind them like the meta bundle.

## 3. Wiring sharpness from the SAM 2nd-pass

The meta-net's input projection consumes `sharpness` per element: `sg2_input_proj_sort` (supergrok2.h:117-146, the `s = sharpness[idx]` term at :133, 141) → in the megakernel, `sg2_stage_input_proj_sorted` reads `sharp[src]` (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:491-516, :505).

Wire `st.sharpness` (the SAM-written `(g_sam−g)²`, opt_components.cuh:193) as the `sharp_base` argument to `sg2_meta_stages` (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:993, 1007). The SAM P2.4 phase writes `st.sharpness[i]=diff*diff` (fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:645) before the meta loop, so it's ready. This is the **same** SAM machinery SG11/15 use (.sam_spec.md); no SAM changes needed beyond adding `OptId::SuperGrok2` to the P2.4 guard (:546-547) and the per-step `do_sam` cadence in `fused_train_step`.

**Note** the standalone binding passes `sharpness_packed` as a separate input (bindings.cpp:1878 arg). In the composed cell, sharpness is produced **in-kernel** by SAM, not passed in — drop the external sharpness arg for the fused path and point `sharp_base` at `st.sharpness`.

## 6. Mamba — LAND DORMANT (do not route)

Mamba is **likely dormant pending the shared-forward race fix in progress** (task brief). The precedent is explicit and load-bearing:
- **mamba×prodigy is BLOCKED: A/A/A determinism FAILS** — the racy block is in the **shared mamba scan/forward**, not the optimizer (dispatch.py:788-805; the kernel is landed-dormant, if-constexpr'd, intentionally NOT registered in `_L3_WGMMA_CELLS`, :1483-1486). Ruled out as state-sizing/smem/barrier; it's the forward itself.
- SG2 on mamba would run the mamba forward **TWICE** (SAM 2nd-pass) **plus** the segmented sort + meta-net register pressure — the .sam_spec.md "Mamba — LAND DORMANT" rule for SG11/15 applies a fortiori (SAM re-runs the mamba forward twice → same A/A/A race as mamba×prodigy).

**Action:** implement mamba×supergrok2 **code-present** (the `mega_mamba_real_adamw_tc_launcher.cu` case + the mamba megakernel SG2 guard) but **GATE OUT of `_L3_WGMMA_CELLS`/`_FUSED_L3_REAL`** until A/A/A is confirmed clean on the fixed mamba forward — exactly the mamba×prodigy carve-out pattern. **Flag in the gate** that SG2's segmented sort + double forward may hit the same mamba-class race (the brief's A/A/A determinism rail).

## 7. HONEST CAVEAT — N&gt;64 CSA fidelity gap (blocks "groks", not "parity")

**Confirmed at source.** The kernel's lightning indexer keeps `qI`/`kI` in **low-rank `indexer_rank` space** and scores with `/sqrt(rank)`:
- `sg2_csa_index_score`: `dot * fast_rsqrt_nr(indexer_rank)` and comment "Scale by 1/sqrt(rank) … (spec §2: / sqrt(d))" — supergrok2.h:217-229 (the `/sqrt(d)`-vs-`/sqrt(rank)` admission is right there).
- `sg2_bilevel_precompute_timestep`: emits the **rank-dim** `qI`, with **`idx_UQ` voided** — supergrok2.h:493-512 (`(void)idx_UQ;` :510, "the indexer score path uses the low-rank form directly").
- The per-op kernel matches this low-rank path (supergrok2_sm90.cuh:250-255; `csa_idx_UQ` commented out at :1556).

But the **fp64 bilevel adjoint ORACLE lifts qI to full `d`** via `idx_UQ` and scores with `/sqrt(d)`:
- `f.qI = torch::mm(torch::mm(x, idx_DQ), idx_UQ)` → **[N, d]**; `f.kI_tok = … idx_UQ` → **[N, d]** — supergrok2_bilevel_adjoint.h:156-157.

So the in-kernel indexer **drops `idx_UQ` and divides by `sqrt(rank)` instead of `sqrt(d)`** → it diverges from the eager-trained meta-net for **N&gt;64** (the top-k selection over compressed entries only bites when `Nc` is large enough that the indexer actually gates, i.e. larger sequences). **Result:**
- ✅ **"L3-TC + parity (single-step) vs the per-op oracle `ops.supergrok2_batched_step`" is achievable** — because the megakernel matches `csa_hca_step_one`, which uses the SAME low-rank indexer (the part-A mirror is 1e-16; the per-op oracle is internally consistent with the kernel).
- ❌ **"Groks" is NOT achievable in this task** — the low-rank indexer diverges from the eager-trained net for N&gt;64; the optimizer won't reproduce the grokking trajectory until `idx_UQ` + `/sqrt(d)` are restored. **This is a separate fix, out of scope here.** Carry this caveat in the deliverable; the gate must include the N&gt;64 CSA fidelity probe (§8) and report it as a known divergence, not a regression.

---

## 8. Gate plan (no suppression)

1. **Per-op parity (the keystone)** — `tests/hw/test_sg2_megakernel.py` part (B), already skip-bodied and referencing `ops.sg2_meta_optimizer_tail` (test :269, :284-286). For the composed cell, add a sibling that runs the **TC megakernel** vs `ops.supergrok2_batched_step` (the oracle) from a shared init:
   - **(B1) single-step:** assert `max|Δ{param, exp_avg, exp_avg_sq, mu, slow, gru_state}| &lt; 1e-5` (test :443-456). The 1e-5 (not 1e-12) tol is the documented parity-hotspot allowance: GRU/PEER are hand-written sequential dots vs cuBLAS, fp32 reorder only (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:132-146; INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:359-364). Transcendentals already match (accurate `expf`/`tanhf` in GRU/PEER; `ptx_expf` in attention to match the reference kernels).
   - **(B2) trajectory:** 200 steps of a fixed grad sequence; assert per-step `mean|param|` proxy + final `|Δparam| &lt; 1e-5` (test :461+).
2. **fp64** — SG-family target ~3e-5, **fp32 storage** (bf16 ~1e-3 would mask bugs). Part (A) fp64 mirror already runs with no GPU at ~1e-16 (test :109-135) and is the primary correctness evidence; keep it as the regression anchor for any meta-net change.
3. **A/A/A determinism (a gate)** — re-run from a bit-identical init; the composed cell must be bit-stable across runs. The meta-net is atomic-free / owner-computes (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:121-130), and the **index-tie-break segmented sort (strategy A) is deterministic by construction**. **FLAG:** SG2's segmented sort + SAM double-forward on **mamba** may hit the shared-mamba-forward race (dispatch.py:788-805) — verify A/A/A on decoder/vit (expected clean), and keep mamba dormant until confirmed.
4. **N&gt;64 CSA fidelity probe** — explicitly compare the kernel's CSA context vs the fp64 bilevel **oracle's** full-`d` `idx_UQ`/`sqrt(d)` indexer for N∈{64, 128, 200} and **report the divergence** as the known, out-of-scope grok blocker (§7) — not a parity failure against `csa_hca_step_one` (which it must still pass).
5. **Single-source math** — every device building block stays called from `supergrok2.h` / the adjoint oracle from `supergrok2_bilevel_adjoint.h` (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:25-29, 481). Do not fork the math into the megakernel.

## 9. Top risks

1. **Sort tie-semantics (highest).** Reproducing torch's unstable CUDA sort order bit-for-bit is not spec'd. Mitigation: strategy A (index tie-break on BOTH the device sort and the oracle perm builder, keeping supergrok2_sm90.cuh:1642 in lock-step) + the tie probe. Getting this wrong silently drifts CSA/HCA over a trajectory (INTEGRATION-NOTES.md:392-400).
2. **Occupancy / smem pressure.** SG2's ~9 KB smem bundle + per-thread topk/peer/gru scratch (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:222-227) on TOP of the decoder/vit DecActs + dW + Muon/Prodigy/SAM scratch. The composed TC cell may drop below occupancy ≥ 1 → the GridBarrier hangs (refuse-if-&lt;1 contract, :1178-1183). Re-measure regs/smem per cell (mega_vit_supergrok2.cu:3 baseline is the L1 surrogate, not the composed TC).
3. **Per-tensor scalars don't fit `FusedScalars`.** SG2's alpha/gru_decay/lamb_eff/beta1/bc1/bc2 are per-tensor (opt_stage_supergrok2.cuh:301-312). Must thread per-tensor scalar buffers via state/workspace (like the meta bundle), not the single global scalar struct — the same representability wall grokadamw's per-tensor clip hit (dispatch.py:1430-1432).
4. **N&gt;64 grok gap (scope honesty).** §7 — single-step parity passes, grok does not. Must be carried as an explicit caveat or the deliverable over-claims.
5. **Mamba A/A/A race.** §6 — land dormant; SAM double-forward + sort likely re-trips the shared-mamba-forward nondeterminism (dispatch.py:788-805). Don't route mamba.
6. **State-buffer sizing / `min_state`.** SG2 needs the largest persisted block (m/v/mu/slow/gru_state + sharpness + perm/unsort + meta bundle + per-tensor scalars). The launcher `min_state` must be bumped or the kernel reads past the buffer (the SG11/15 sizing note, dispatch.cpp:772+).

## Summary

I wired host-side SharpnessMetaNet training into the L3 path of `train_supergrok`/`train_supergrok15`, mirroring `train_neuralgrok`'s amplifier-training pattern exactly. SG11/SG15 now run as **real SuperGrok** (rescale ≠ 0, mu ≠ 0) instead of degenerating to ≈AdamW.

### The change (committed: `97b070e`, host-side Python only, no rebuild)
File: `/workspace/SuperGrok1.5/grokking_race_v2.py` (+84/−14, single file).

The L3 branches previously **early-returned before any side-step**, so the meta-net was never trained on the L3 path (`rescale` stayed at init 0 → mu ≈ 0). The fix, on the meta/SAM side-step cadence:
1. Runs an eager fwd/bwd to **seed `p.grad`** (the `|g|`/sharpness featurization the meta-net + SAM read — the autograd pass the megakernel doesn't return, exactly like NeuralGrok's eager seed).
2. Runs `sam_step` (→ `_flat_sharpness`) then `meta_step`/`bilevel_step` (the val+train lookahead that **trains** the meta-net; sets `_weights_dirty`, restores `p.grad`).
3. Launches the L3 kernel. `fused_train_step` already re-extracts the fresh phi pack into the kernel's `extra` slice and reads the live `sg_rescale` every step (the existing SuperGrok11/15 SEAM in `dispatch.py`) — the SG analogue of NeuralGrok's `psi_pack` re-extraction.

**Kernel math unchanged.** No `.cu/.cuh/.h`/`dispatch.py` edits. The in-kernel gate/alpha scalars (`_cached_train_acc`/`_cached_alpha`) are deliberately **not** fed on the L3 path, so single-step parity is preserved — only the meta-net *weights* move, flowing through the already-live phi pack + `sg_rescale` read.

### Raw evidence — rescale/mu now MOVE (vs staying 0)
600-step probe, decoder, bf16 L3-TC wgmma, seed 42, production configs:

| | LAST_L3_ENGINE | host meta forwards | rescale (first→last) | ‖mu‖ (first→last) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **SG1.1** | wgmma/decoder/bf16 ✓ | 120 (muf=5) | `0.0 → +1.162e-02` | `0.0 → 9.85e-02` |
| **SG1.5** | wgmma/decoder/bf16 ✓ | 20 (bilevel_freq) | `0.0 → +1.611e-03` | `0.0 → 1.486e-03` |

Both confirm the **real L3 megakernel ran** and **rescale moved off 0 → mu nonzero** (was the degenerate `rescale=0 ⇒ mu≡0 ≈AdamW`).

### Multi-step trajectory (real-SG dynamics — honest grok note)
Both exhibit the documented **memorize-then-collapse**: train climbs 0.011→0.996 (memorizes ~step 175-200, with destabilizing dips — SG11: 0.897/0.847/0.727; SG15: 0.945/0.661 — as the now-active meta pushes off the minimum), while **val/test stay pinned at ~0.019/~0.011** and **`grokking_step=None`**. **They do NOT grok.** This is the faithful RESULT (fidelity, not grokking); the meta stays fully active, the collapse is reported, not suppressed.

### Kernel + tail-gate unchanged (33-cell gate)
- Dedicated SG pytest gate **with my change**: `supergrok11/{decoder,vit,mamba}` + `supergrok15/{decoder,vit,mamba}` → **6 passed**, A/A/A-deterministic, in 74s.

Build phase is now **fully closed**: 33/33 cells L3-TC, SG11 sigmoid-gated, SG11/15 host-trained as real SG, all parity/A-A-A green. Everything from here is the performance work — so let's settle the scale, because I think it's the crux you're circling.

## The metric and the scale are coupled

Roofline % measures how well the kernel saturates the tensor cores — and that's only a *movable* number when the GEMMs are big enough to feed them:

- **At d=128 (the actual race):** the decoder runs seq=4, so the forward GEMM is **M=4 rows** into a wgmma instruction that wants **64-row tiles** → ~94% of the tensor-core throughput is wasted on padding before you optimize anything. The GEMM is *smaller than a single tile*, so tiling / split-K / multi-CTA have nothing to bite on. The roofline % is low **and nearly immovable** — a hill-climb there chases noise. The real lever at this scale is **wall-clock per race**, dominated by launch overhead, occupancy, host↔device syncs, and how much of the model is fused in — *not* tensor-core throughput.
- **At d=2048:** GEMMs are large, the tensor cores can actually be saturated, % climbs toward 70–90% with good work, and all the throughput levers move the number. This is where "maximize the roofline" is a genuine objective.

So **"use the actual race" and "maximize the roofline test" pull in opposite directions** — the actual race is exactly the scale where the roofline number won't budge.

## The question underneath: what's the optimization *for*?

- **(A) Cheaper real experiments** — the science runs at toy scale, so to cut *real* instance-hours you optimize the d=128 race **wall-clock** (launch overhead, occupancy, fusion, syncs). Metric = race seconds, not roofline %. Directly reduces what you pay for actual runs.
- **(B) A demonstrably peak-performance portable megakernel** (engineering artifact) — optimize **d=2048 roofline %**. Proves the kernels saturate an H100 and port cleanly, but doesn't speed up the real experiments.
- **(C) Both.**

## My recommendation — a hybrid that fits "fewest instance hours" + "the actual race"

The highest-value levers — occupancy, register pressure, launch overhead, host-sync removal, deeper fusion — help **both**: they cut the real d=128 race wall-clock *and* lift the d=2048 roofline %, and they're the portable wins. Only the big-GEMM-specific levers (tiling/split-K/multi-CTA) are d=2048-only. So:

1. First hill-climb the **shared** levers on the **real d=128 race wall-clock** → cuts the instance-hours you actually pay, *is* "the actual race," portable.
2. *Then*, if you want the high roofline-% showcase, scale to d=2048 for the big-GEMM-only levers.

Real savings first, the impressive number second, in cost order.

---

So — **what's driving it for you: cheaper real runs, a peak-performance showcase, or both?** And are you anchored on roofline-% specifically (→ d=2048), or is "make the real race faster" (wall-clock at d=128) the actual goal? That one call decides the whole hill-climb.

That settles it cleanly — and your early-stop reasoning is right: bigger models tend to grok with *less* delay (the memorize→generalize gap shrinks as capacity grows), so early stopping caps the wall-clock even as per-step cost rises. Scaling the real models instead of a throwaway benchmark config is the better call: same portable code path, real science at non-toy scale, and a roofline number that actually means something.

**"Just d?" — d is the primary lever, but it travels with two others:**
- **d (dim_model): yes, the main knob.** FFN/attention GEMM FLOPs grow ~d², and d sets the K/N of every GEMM — exactly what feeds the tensor cores. Scaling d is what moves the roofline.
- **batch: the other half.** It sets M (GEMM rows) and amortizes overhead — at toy sequence lengths, batch is what gives you full 64-row+ tensor-core tiles. That's the separate sweep (#16); d and batch *together* define the workload.
- **head_dim ≥ 64** — keep heads = d/128 (d=2048 → 16 heads × 128) so the attention GEMMs stay wgmma-friendly.
- **depth (n_layers): scale modestly for credibility, but it's roofline-neutral** — more layers = more *same-size* GEMMs at the same efficiency + linear wall-clock. It makes the model "real," it doesn't move the metric.

**"How much?" — target d=2048.** The sweet spot: GPT-2-medium width, GEMMs big enough that the roofline climbs into a genuinely meaningful range and every lever (tiling/split-K/multi-CTA/TMA-pipelining) bites. Ladder: d=512 starts to matter, d=1024 is a solid "real small-LM," **d=2048 is where the engineering shows**, d=4096 goes HBM/occupancy-bound for little extra headroom and a lot more pain. So 2048, not higher.

**The catch worth knowing up front — this promotes multi-CTA tiling to the critical path.** The current megakernel pins one CTA per SM, and d=2048 breaks that: vit/mamba's scalar smem blows past the 228 KB/SM ceiling → the launcher refuses; decoder runs but goes serial-per-CTA + register/HBM-bound (low roofline). So **d=2048 isn't runnable today** — it needs the multi-CTA-per-tensor tiling infra (task #14). That's actually good news: #14 is simultaneously what *unlocks* larger d **and** the single biggest roofline lever we have. It just moves from "later" to "first."

**One honesty flag on the science:** d=2048 on mod-97 (~9.4k examples) is extreme overparameterization — a giant model on a tiny table. It'll likely still grok, but weight decay (the load-bearing grokking knob) will need retuning, and to genuinely prove *grokking* at non-toy scale rather than a big model memorizing a small set, you'd want to scale the **task** too — a larger prime (p≈251–509 → 63k–260k examples). That lengthens the grok delay (early-stop only fires once it groks), so it's a real wall-clock trade. My rec: bump the prime moderately (~251) alongside d so the claim is honest.

2. Add the scaled preset (d, heads, layers, prime) + a short **grok-sanity** run with early-stop → confirms it still groks and retunes weight decay.

Then we lock the number → build #14 → baseline → hill-climb.
