## Your reviewer instinct is exactly right — and it's the most important point in this whole thread

Scaling **only** d *is* a flag, on three counts:

**1. Aspect ratio (the giveaway).** d=2048 at the current nl=2 is a **1024:1 width-to-depth** model — a "pancake" nobody would ever train, transparently shaped to inflate GEMM size and nothing else. Real models live at **33–43:1** (GPT-2 medium 43:1, large 36:1, XL 33:1). A reviewer sees 1024:1 and immediately reads "they tuned the architecture to flatter the hardware metric." The fix is to **scale depth with width** so you land on a recognizable shape — d=1024/nl=24 *is* GPT-2-medium exactly; d=2048 wants nl≈48 (GPT-2-XL proportions) to sit at ~43:1.

**2. A single hand-picked size.** Reporting roofline at one (d, batch) that happens to tile perfectly is gameable. The credible thing is a **roofline curve across sizes** + absolute TFLOP/s + wall-clock — which also happens to defuse flag #1 by showing you didn't cherry-pick.

**3. Overparam vs. the task (the science flag).** 0.3–1.2B params on a mod-p table is a giant model on a tiny dataset — a reviewer dismisses any "grokking at scale" claim unless the **task** scales too. That's why I'd bump the prime (p≈251–509); the table shows it costs ~nothing in params.

The reassuring part: every fix that defuses a flag is also just *good practice* — and notice the roofline doesn't actually *need* d=2048. d=1024 already feeds the tensor cores well; d=2048's edge is mostly Amdahl (GEMMs dominate the fused kernel's wall more) and arithmetic intensity. So "max out d" is a **headline** choice, not a necessity.

## So my recommendation shifts slightly

Scale **d and depth together** at a constant ~GPT-2 aspect ratio, and report a **curve**, not a point:

- **Credible anchor: d=1024, nl=24 — literally GPT-2-medium (~0.30B, 43:1).** Non-toy, textbook-standard shape, feeds the tensor cores well, ~4× cheaper than the 1.2B option, fewest instance-hours.
- **Stretch headline (optional): d=2048, nl≈24–48 (1.2–2.4B).** Bigger roofline ceiling and a louder number, but 4–8× the params/hours.
- **Prime → ~251** either way, for the science claim.

One engineering reality regardless of which you pick: vit/mamba already sit at ~184 KB smem at d=128, so **any** real scale-up blows the 228 KB/SM ceiling on the current one-CTA kernel — **multi-CTA tiling (#14) is the prerequisite**, and it's also the biggest roofline lever, so that's the first build either way.

### ASSISTANT [2026-06-12T01:09:10]
Verified now — and the answer to your middle question is the important one, so let me be precise with the code evidence.

## 1. Default size options

There's **one shared width/depth schema**, not per-model ones — `MODEL_SCALES` (grokking_race_v2.py:231):

| preset | dim_model | heads | layers | ~params |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **small** *(the default)* | 128 | 4 | 2 | ~0.42M |
| medium | 256 | 8 | 4 | ~3.5M |
| large | 512 | 8 | 6 | ~20M |

Default everywhere is **small / d=128 / nl=2**. What actually *differs* between the 3 models isn't width/depth — it's the **task-shaped dims**: decoder (vocab=99, seq=4), vit (patch_dim=49, num_patches=16 → seq=17), mamba (seq_len=8, chain_length=3, expand=2, state=16 → a structurally different block, hence ~half the params). So they share the size knobs but sit on different tasks.

## 2. You're right — it *is* the megakernel, not PyTorch. Here's the exact split.

The L3-TC megakernel runs the **real forward + backward + optimizer in one persistent CUDA kernel** and **replaces** eager PyTorch for that step — confirmed in the source:
- `fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:6`: *"ONE persistent kernel per training step runs the REAL transformer-decoder forward+backward AND the optimizer math"*
- `dispatch.py:591`: the L3-REAL path *"REPLACES the eager fwd+bwd+opt for that one cell."*
- `roofline.py` times exactly this — `mod.tc_train_step` (the wgmma megakernel), not PyTorch.

**PyTorch's only roles** are: (a) *defining* the architecture — the weight tensors and init; (b) the eager fp64/fp32 **parity reference** the megakernel is checked against; (c) eval-forward for accuracy. I used it **purely to count parameters** — a static property of the architecture. The megakernel transcribes the *identical* weights/math (`model_stages_decoder.cuh`), so the param counts are exactly the megakernel's too. We are not training in PyTorch on the L3 path.

**The catch that makes scaling real work:** the megakernel's dims are **compile-time constants** (codegen'd layout headers, SSOT `megakernel_codegen.py`), and it's currently built at **d=128**. Scaling it to d=1024/2048 isn't a runtime flag like it is for the PyTorch model — it means **regenerating the kernel at the new dims** (+ updating the oracle in lockstep, which `static_assert`s enforce) **plus the multi-CTA infra (#14)** to clear the smem ceiling. That asymmetry — config-flag for PyTorch vs. codegen+rebuild for the megakernel — is the actual engineering.

The codebase even quantifies *why* d=128 is the wrong place to optimize: `roofline.py:97` — *"megakernel saturates ~8× below … occupancy-pinned … within 3.5% of its peak there."* It's already at its *own* ceiling at d=128; that ceiling is just low. Scaling d is what lifts it.

## 3. What size proves it "scales well"

Not a single size — **the efficiency curve staying high as you scale.** A reviewer is convinced by roofline % (and absolute TFLOP/s) measured across a **non-toy range** and shown to hold or rise as d grows — anchored at a recognized size:

- **Floor for "not a toy":** ~100M params. Below that it reads as small.
- **Unambiguous anchor:** GPT-2-medium class — **d=1024, nl=24, ~0.30B**. Canonical shape, nobody calls it toy.
- **The actual proof:** a 3–4 point curve, e.g. **d=512 (~75M) → d=1024 (~300M) → d=2048 (~1.2B)**, showing the megakernel holds a high % across the range. One high number at one size proves nothing; a flat/rising curve through ~300M–1B does.

And concretely, given §2: **each point on that curve is a separate codegen + build of the megakernel at those dims** (the big ones needing #14). So "prove it scales" literally *is* the roofline-curve-across-sizes work — which is why multi-CTA tiling (#14) is the first build regardless of where you set the top.

So the honest framing: **d=1024/nl=24 (~300M) is the smallest size that *proves* it**, and you'd show a curve up to d=2048 (~1.2B) for the headline. Want me to set the curve at {512, 1024, 2048} (GPT-2-family aspect ratios, prime→251) and start with #14 so the megakernel can actually reach those dims?

## The three things this tells us

**1. The 80 GB wall is optimizer-dependent — that's the real ceiling, not params.** A single H100 caps at:
- **Heavy-state cells (SG2, SG11/15 — meta-net + SAM scratch):** ~**4xl / GPT-2-XL (1.5B)**, which fits at 65 GB; 5xl OOMs.
- **Lean cells (adamw, lion, grokfast, muon…):** **6xl / GPT-3-2.7B class (2.5B)**, ~45 GB.
- **Mamba** (~half the params) isn't memory-limited anywhere on this ladder — even SG2 fits at 6xl.
- Past that needs multi-GPU sharding — out of scope for the single-H100 campaign (a thing for when mi300x/multi-H100 come online).

**2. The GPT-2/GPT-3 anchors make it bulletproof.** xxl *is* GPT-2-medium, 4xl *is* GPT-2-XL. "All 33 megakernel cells at GPT-2-XL scale, lean cells to GPT-3-2.7B, on one H100" is a claim no reviewer waves off.

**3. ### ASSISTANT [2026-06-12T01:34:38]
8×H100 genuinely changes the calculus — and the most valuable use is the *cheapest* one. Two very different things it unlocks:

## A. Speed (free — zero new kernel work)
The roofline curve (6 sizes) × grok demos × 33 cells is otherwise **serial**. Shard those independent jobs across 8 GPUs → **~8× faster wall-clock**. Since 8 GPUs bill at 8× the rate but finish in ~1/8 the time, **total cost stays roughly flat** and you get results 8× sooner. This is a no-brainer — I'd use the 8 GPUs this way regardless of anything else, and it directly serves the instance-hours goal.

## B. Size (real engineering): cross the 80 GB wall
640 GB aggregate. With **optimizer-state sharding (ZeRO-style)**, the ceiling jumps from ~1.5–2.5B (single-GPU) to roughly **~10B for the heavy-state cells (SG2), ~20–35B for the lean ones** (with headroom). That extends the ladder into GPT-3-6.7B (7xl) and GPT-3-13B (8xl) territory.

## The architecture rule that protects the project: wrap the megakernel, don't split it
- **Data-parallel + ZeRO** keeps the persistent megakernel *intact* — each GPU runs the full fwd+bwd+opt megakernel on a batch/state shard, and the NCCL collectives (reduce-scatter grads, all-gather weights) happen *around* it, between steps. Single-GPU-per-step, still portable. ✅
- **Tensor/model parallelism** needs cross-GPU all-reduce *inside* the forward/backward (after every parallel GEMM) → that breaks the one-kernel-per-step design into many kernels with NCCL between them, destroying the megakernel that is the entire point. ❌

So multi-GPU should be a **thin distributed layer around** the megakernel, never a redesign of it.

## Honest take on how big to actually go
Per-GPU roofline % is already near its ceiling at GPT-2-XL (1.5B) — the GEMMs saturate the tensor cores there, and 7B/13B don't raise the *per-GPU* % much. What multi-GPU genuinely adds is (1) speed, and (2) a strong new result on top: **near-linear scaling efficiency 1→8 GPUs** (low comm overhead). Past ~2.7B you're mostly buying a louder headline, not a higher %.

## Recommendation
- **Now:** 8 GPUs parallelize the sweep — the ≤2.5B ladder, 8× faster.
- **Next workstream (after #14):** add the DP+ZeRO layer around the megakernel → fit *all* heavy-state cells at GPT-2-XL/GPT-3-2.7B, push lean cells to ~5–7B (GPT-3-6.7B) as the multi-GPU headline, and report the 1→8 scaling curve.
- **Don't** do tensor parallelism.

**The fork for you:** is the 8×H100 mainly for **speed** (parallelize the existing ladder — cheap, immediate), **size** (build the DP+ZeRO layer, push to 5–13B — a real project), or **both**? And where's the top — GPT-2-XL (1.5B, one cell per GPU, just parallelized) or stretch to GPT-3-6.7B/13B via sharding?

Front of the build order is unchanged either way: **#14 (single-GPU multi-CTA) is still first** — every scale-up and the multi-GPU layer both sit on top of it.

### ASSISTANT [2026-06-12T01:36:31]
Good catch — batch is the **M-dimension** of every GEMM, so it's co-equal with d for the roofline, and it's *especially* load-bearing here because the sequences are tiny (decoder seq = **4**). Five things it changes:

**1. Batch fills the tensor-core M-tiles.** wgmma works in 64-row M-tiles, and M = batch × seq. At seq=4 you need batch in the hundreds just to fill a few tiles per SM. Too-small batch → low roofline % *no matter how big d is*. There's a **saturation knee** per (model, d): below it the cores starve, above it you just burn memory for zero % gain. That knee is exactly what #16 sweeps.

**2. The knee moves with d.** At d=128 the GEMMs are so small the kernel needs a *huge* batch (roofline.py measured ~16k — and was *still* 8× under peak because it's occupancy-pinned: the #14 problem). At large d + multi-CTA, big GEMMs saturate at a *much smaller* batch. So the knee comes **down** as the model grows — which is convenient, because…

**3. …batch competes with optimizer state for the 80 GB.** Memory = optimizer-state (∝ params) **+ activations (∝ batch × d × layers)** — my earlier table was optimizer-state-only. At the big tiers the heavy cells' state dominates (SG2 @ 4xl = 65 GB), leaving little for activations → caps batch → which can re-starve M. So at large d the heavy-state cells get squeezed on batch — another reason they top out lower, and another thing ZeRO-sharding relieves (it frees memory back for batch).

**4. Batch couples to the grokking science — not a free knob.** On these small datasets (mod-97 ≈ 4.7k train) a roofline-saturating batch is a big fraction of the data → near-full-batch, which shifts the grok dynamics. Same split as the d-scale: **saturation-knee batch for the roofline measurement, grok-appropriate batch for the science demo** — two configs, not one.

**5. Multi-GPU *is* batch scaling.** Data-parallel grows the **global** batch (each GPU runs a local shard). Weak-scaling — local batch pinned at the per-GPU knee, global = 8 × knee — keeps every GPU's roofline % high and is the right mode for the curve. It also lets you assemble a large global batch for the science while each GPU stays at its efficient local size.

**So batch becomes a per-tier swept axis, not one locked number.** Concretely: generalize #16 from "lock one batch at d=2048" → "find the saturation knee at each ladder tier"; fold batch into the hill-climb's search space (it interacts with the tile dims in #12 — optimal tile depends on M depends on batch); run the science demos at a separate grok-appropriate global batch assembled via DP. Under multi-GPU, weak-scale so per-GPU local batch = the knee.

Since you're doing the full NCCL work: **data-parallel + ZeRO-3 optimizer/weight sharding, wrapping the megakernel** (collectives between steps, kernel stays one-per-step). **No tensor or pipeline parallelism** — those need cross-GPU sync *inside* fwd/bwd and would shatter the single-kernel design that is the whole asset. That forces you to dismantle the one-kernel-per-step megakernel into per-block kernels with NCCL between them — **destroying the novel asset to bolt on conventional plumbing you don't need.** Sequence-parallel (the usual 4th D) is moot here: seq is 4–17, nothing to split. Pipeline-parallel is the only one the megakernel tolerates, and it only matters past ~13B — scientifically silly for mod-arithmetic.

The *publishable* systems result here is "**a fused fwd+bwd+opt megakernel kept intact while scaled to 6.7B at near-linear 8-GPU efficiency.**" That's harder and more novel than a Megatron-style 4D loop. Don't trade it away.

**AMD + TPU — straight: defer them, your instinct is right.** They're portability *polish*, not core value. The code paths already exist (gfx942 HIP, Pallas), so "portable by design" is already a true claim. But standing up real mi300x/TPU hardware and getting roofline parity on each is a separate project per vendor, with real cost. **One vendor done maximally beats three done partially.** Keep the paths maintained (cheap), but don't burn hardware/effort on them until H100 is fully locked — then decide with results in hand.

**The thread tying these together:** the strongest version of this is **deep, not wide** — H100, megakernel intact, DP+ZeRO to 6.7B, hill-climbed to its roofline ceiling, grokking shown at GPT-2-medium. Both 4D parallelism and the 3-vendor spread pull *against* that — they'd dilute a sharp, novel result into a broader, more conventional one. **My honest vote: cut both, go deep.**

### ASSISTANT [2026-06-12T03:39:36]
Several of these are sharp and worth precise answers — including correcting where ZeRO-3 actually lands.

**ZeRO-2, deliberately — not ZeRO-3 + SP. The megakernel is the dividing line:**
- **ZeRO-2** shards optimizer state + gradients; **params stay resident.** The megakernel runs the whole model's fwd+bwd+opt in one launch (all weights present), and the reduce-scatter/all-gather happens at the step boundary, *around* the kernel. Compatible. Fits ~13B/GPU for lean cells on 8×H100.
- **ZeRO-3** also shards **params**, all-gathering each layer's weights *just-in-time during the forward.* For a whole-model megakernel that's the same poison as tensor-parallel — you'd either gather the full model up front (no memory saving, defeating ZeRO-3) or gather per-layer *inside* the kernel (mid-launch cross-GPU comm, breaks the single kernel). So **ZeRO-2 is the highest sharding level a whole-model megakernel allows** — and at ≤13B you don't need ZeRO-3 anyway (resident bf16 weights @13B = 26 GB/GPU).
- **SP is moot here.** Sequence-parallel shards activations along the *sequence* axis for long-context memory. Our seq is **4 / 17 / 8** — nothing to shard, and SP is normally bolted to TP, which we're avoiding.

So it's **DP + ZeRO-2**, purely a function of what the kernel can swallow.

**"Parallelism as a portable wrapper/config around the megakernel, single→multi-GPU" — that's exactly the right instinct, and it's the clincher.** A parallelism layer can be a clean wrapper *only if it never reaches inside the kernel.* DP + ZeRO-2 is precisely that: single-GPU → the collectives are no-ops; multi-GPU → same megakernel + collectives at the step boundary. One code path, scales by config. **TP and PP can't be wrappers** — they need the kernel to run partial models or talk mid-compute, i.e. surgery on the kernel. So your own "must be a portable wrapper" requirement is itself the argument for bounding the parallelism to DP+ZeRO — it's the only kind that *is* a wrapper. That's the portable single+multi-GPU layer you're after.

**H100-only now, AMD/TPU code parked for later — good, that sharpens everything.** And we finalize the H100 reference first regardless, so deferring the ports costs nothing.

**Dataset complexity — your instinct is correct, and it's a real grokking constraint, not a nicety.** A 6B model on mod-97 (~4.7k train) is ~1000× over-capacity → it memorizes instantly and grokking goes trivial/degenerate. To keep grokking *meaningful* you scale the **task with the model.** Levers, cleanest first:
- **Larger prime p:** 97 → 251 / 509 / 997 → dataset = p² (63k / 259k / 994k), and more residues = genuinely harder generalization. Primary lever; same task, just bigger.
- **Lower train fraction:** 50% → ~30–40% → less data to infer the rule → sharper, later grokking. Pure-difficulty knob, no task change (below a critical fraction it won't grok at all — so it's empirical).
- **Mamba: longer chains** (3 → 4+); **vit** is anchored to MNIST, so p is its arithmetic knob.

Two caveats: it's **empirical** (you can't predict the prime/fraction that groks cleanly at a given size — the grok-sanity runs sweep it), and it should **co-scale with the ladder** (a 1.5B grok demo needs a harder task than a 300M one). And it only touches the **science** runs — the roofline measurement doesn't care about the dataset, just the kernel.

Net: H100-only + DP/ZeRO-2-as-wrapper + a scaled task is actually *simpler* than where we were an hour ago. The one real decision left before I lock it and start #14: **uniform flagship size for all three, or decoder-led with vit/mamba held lower?** (Task hardness I'll just sweep empirically, but if you have a target prime in mind, say so.)

- Good news: the megakernel **already** separates those phases with a grid barrier, so this is cutting at a seam that exists, not a redesign. The fwd+bwd fusion survives; the opt just becomes a separate, shard-aware launch.
- Honest consequence: **full fwd+bwd+opt fusion is a single-GPU property.** Single-GPU = the purest artifact (all three fused); multi-GPU = fused fwd+bwd megakernel + a separate sharded optimizer, ZeRO-2 comm between. Both are novel (a fused fwd+bwd megakernel is already unusual), but "one kernel does everything" is specifically the *single-GPU* claim.

So vit's task-complexity, input geometry, and kernel relayout are coupled in a way the other two aren't.

- **Plus a convergence ceiling:** very large batches train worse per-sample and shift the grokking dynamics — so the *useful* batch for the **science** sits below the throughput-saturating batch.

### ASSISTANT [2026-06-12T04:28:00]
Fair — you asked me to actually answer the comparison, not lock your pick. So here's my genuine head-to-head, **4D (TP+DP+PP+SP)+ZeRO-3 vs DP+ZeRO-2, both at max batch**, no hedging.

**The organizing principle:** every parallelism dimension beyond DP+ZeRO-2 is *overhead you add only when memory forces you.* At a size the simpler config already fits, more dimensions = **lower** MFU, never higher. Dimension by dimension, at a shared size:
- **TP** splits each GEMM across GPUs → smaller per-GPU GEMMs (worse tensor-core efficiency) + an all-reduce per block. Throughput **down.**
- **PP** adds pipeline bubbles (idle fill/drain) + inter-stage activation comm. Throughput **down.**
- **ZeRO-3** vs ZeRO-2 adds a weight all-gather every layer. Throughput **down.**
- **SP** isn't even close: it shards the *sequence* dimension, which exists to tame **thousand-to-million-token** activations. Our sequences are **4 / 17 / 8.** There is nothing to shard — SP contributes exactly zero. The "4D" is 3D at best for this workload.

And **max batch makes this worse for 4D, not better:** a big batch is exactly what saturates each GPU under DP (full M-tiles, high MFU). 4D spends GPUs splitting the *model* instead of processing more of that batch — so at max batch, DP+ZeRO-2 pulls *further* ahead.

**The only thing 4D+ZeRO-3 buys is size past ~13B.** ZeRO-3 shards weights, TP/PP split the model, so you reach the **~20–30B** ceiling 8×H100 allows — which DP+ZeRO-2 simply can't fit. So the whole comparison collapses to:
- **≤13B: DP+ZeRO-2 is faster *and* simpler. 4D loses outright** (overhead for a model that didn't need splitting).
- **13–30B: 4D+ZeRO-3 is *necessary* — not faster, just the only thing that fits.**

4D never *beats* DP+ZeRO-2 on throughput at a shared size; it only *enables* sizes DP can't reach, at *lower* per-GPU MFU.

**So the real question isn't "which is faster" — it's "do you need to exceed 13B?"** My honest read: **no — I'd take DP+ZeRO-2.** GPT-3-13B is already an absurd scale for grokking mod-arithmetic *and* a flagship-worthy fused-megakernel result. 20–30B adds no scientific value, runs at *lower* MFU, costs a frontier-risk build (device-TP via NVSHMEM + PP scheduling), and drags in SP that does literally nothing at seq 4–17.

The one case 4D wins: if the accomplishment you want is **"we ran full 4D at the 8×H100 ceiling"** as a systems flex in itself — a bigger *number*, not better *performance.* Legitimate to want, but that's a different goal than the "max performance" you named, and it's where the cost and risk live.

**My recommendation: DP+ZeRO-2, max batch, push to 13B.** Higher throughput, far less risk, still flagship scale — and I'd keep the template 4D-*capable* so a future >8-GPU run can slot in TP/PP/ZeRO-3 without a redesign. I did **not** implement forward-GEMM split-K, because it is neither safe, minimal, nor beneficial here — the task's own guardrail ("prefer reverting over weakening any gate; if it can't be made A/A/A-clean or breaks the design, report what blocks it") points to a report:

1. **No idle SMs to fill.** The forward is already multi-CTA via token (M) tiling and runs at 0.99 waves at production B. Splitting a forward GEMM's K across CTAs targets a bottleneck that does not exist there. (Confirms HANDOFF.md: "megakernels saturate at B≈2k … max batch helps GEMM size but not occupancy. Multi-CTA-**per-tensor** tiling [= the dW split-K, P2] is the open lever.")

2. **The split-K pattern is structurally incompatible with the forward.** dW split-K works *only* because dW lives in **P2 — a separate phase after grid barrier B1**, where all activations are materialized in HBM and SMs are genuinely idle. A forward GEMM's output is consumed **immediately, in the same CTA, in the same barrier-free tile loop** (e.g. `dectc_gemm_fwd → +bias → attention → out_proj`, `model_stage_decoder_tc.cuh:693-744`). Cooperative cross-CTA K-split there would require a **per-layer grid barrier** to let the owning CTA wait on the cooperators' partials — which the design **explicitly rejects** (header line 21-23: "barrier-free within the tile (no per-layer grid barrier — DESIGN explicitly rejects that)"). It would convert ~2 barriers/step into ~50 and serialize every tile at every layer. A guarded forward-split-K would never be exercised and adds risk for zero benefit.

**What would actually lift the cap (next real lever, out of scope for "smallest safe increment"):** the ceiling binds in the **small-batch regime (B ≲ 4k → n_tiles &lt; 132)** and in P2. The faithful continuation is **multi-CTA-per-tile forward cooperation** that activates *only* when `n_tiles &lt; n_sms` — assigning spare CTAs to split a tile's GEMM K with a deterministic reduce — which necessarily reintroduces the per-tile barrier the design rejected, so it is a **design change, not a minimal increment**, and must be its own task with its own parity campaign. The already-shipped dW/mamba/vit split-K (G=4) is the correct and complete realization of the multi-CTA-per-tensor lever for the phase that has idle SMs.

---

# FIT-CEILING REPORT — Task #14 Phase-1 diagnostic (single H100, sm_90a)

## Headline finding (REFUTES the premise)
The L3-TC megakernel's **shared memory is essentially independent of model width `d`** for all three models. The activations are staged through **HBM workspace** (Fork B design), so the only smem the `*_megakernel_tc` kernels use is a tiny static wgmma staging arena (one A(64×16) + one B(N×16) bf16 tile + a 256-float reduction + the dW specs). None of those terms reference `SG_*_D`. **No model hits a smem cliff from width scaling on the TC path — not vit, not mamba, not decoder.** The "vit/mamba cliff earlier than decoder" claim is true only for the *scalar* fallback kernels, which are a separate code path (see below).

## Per-model FIT-CEILING table (ptxas-measured, `-arch=compute_90a --use_fast_math -Xptxas -v`, nvcc 12.4)

The L3-TC kernel = `fused_{decoder,vit,mamba}_megakernel_tc&lt;AdamW&gt;`, 256 threads/CTA, persistent 1-CTA/SM. occ = min(65536/(regs·256), 233472/smem).

```
model      d   | TC regs  TC smem    TC spill  occ  | TC FITS?  blocker
decoder   128  |   128    13748 B    0 B        2   | YES       —
decoder   768  |   164    13748 B    0 B        1   | YES       —
decoder  1024  |   128    13748 B    0 B        2   | YES       —
decoder  1280  |   164    13748 B    0 B        1   | YES       —
decoder  1600  |   166    13748 B    0 B        1   | YES       —
decoder  2048  |   128    13748 B    0 B        2   | YES       —
vit       128  |   255     7664 B    6432 B     1   | YES       — (spills, but fits)
vit       768  |   255     7664 B    7820 B     1   | YES       —
vit      1024  |   255     7664 B    8836 B     1   | YES       —
vit      1280  |   255     7664 B   10500 B     1   | YES       —
vit      1600  |   255     7664 B   12648 B     1   | YES       —
vit      2048  |   255     7664 B   14792 B     1   | YES       —
mamba     128  |   255    14800 B    20 B       1   | YES       —
mamba     768  |   255    14800 B    20 B       1   | YES       —
mamba    1024  |   255    14800 B    20 B       1   | YES       —
mamba    1280  |   255    14800 B    20 B       1   | YES       —
mamba    1600  |   255    14800 B    20 B       1   | YES       —
mamba    2048  |   255    14800 B    20 B       1   | YES       —
```

**Exact ceiling `d` per model (TC path):** NONE within {768…2048}. All three TC megakernels FIT at every d up to 2048 (smem flat and ≪ 228 KB; occ ≥ 1; they compile). The TC megakernel smem footprint does not begin to be the #14 problem anywhere in this ladder.

## The real early cliff is the SCALAR co-compiled kernel (this is what "cliffs")
Each TC TU also compiles the model's **scalar** megakernel (`fused_*_megakernel`, the per-sample `*SampleSmem` path), whose smem DOES grow with d. This is where the d=768 wall actually is, and it is the genuine reason a naive width bump fails to *build the TU* today:

```
            scalar smem (KB)         cap &amp; verdict
   d    decoder      vit      mamba   | decoder=STATIC&lt;=48KB | vit/mamba=DYNAMIC&lt;=227KB/224KB
  128    41.9       183.7    141.7    | all OK (these == the committed header constants)
  768   241.9      1033.7    823.0    | ALL OVER — decoder hits ptxas hard-stop (&gt;164KB),
 1024   321.9      1373.7   1095.5    |   vit/mamba trip their own &lt;227KB/&lt;224KB static_asserts
 ...    (grows linearly in d)         |
 2048   641.9      2733.7   2185.5    |
```
So on the SCALAR path, vit cliffs hardest and earliest (largest seq×dff working set), mamba next, decoder last in absolute KB — but decoder has the *tightest cap* (48 KB static vs 227 KB dynamic), so decoder-scalar actually fails its budget at the smallest *relative* growth. All three scalar paths break at d=768. **This scalar path is dead weight for the production wgmma route** (`wiring_check` shows 33/33 run the TC engine, never the scalar one); decoupling it from the TC TU is itself a candidate #14 cleanup.

## Where #14 footprint work actually begins
Not smem. The width-sensitive resource on the TC path is **register spilling**, and only for vit:
- **decoder TC**: 0 spills at every d — clean. 128–166 regs.
- **mamba TC**: flat 20 B spill at every d, but pinned at **255 regs (occ exactly 1, zero margin)** — one more register and occupancy drops to 0. Mamba is register-bound, not smem-bound.
- **vit TC**: spills climb 6.4 KB → 14.8 KB as d 128 → 2048 (local-memory traffic, not a fit blocker but an efficiency drag), also at 255 regs / occ 1.

## smem(d) formula (validated to the byte)
**TC path (the deliverable kernels), d-INDEPENDENT:**
- `DecTcSmem`  = stages·64·16·2 + stages·N·16·2 + 256·4 + 9·sizeof(DecDwSpec) → 13744 B analytic / **13748 B measured** (stages=2, N=128).
- `VitTcSmem`  = 64·16·2 + N·16·2 + 256·4 + 10·48 → 7648 B / **7664 B measured** (no stages factor).

## Summary

Wrote `/workspace/.parallelism_design.md` — the Phase-2 contract for the 4D+ZeRO-3 metaprogrammed parallelism layer over the L3-TC megakernel. CPU-only, no repo edits, no build, no GPU work (verified via `git status` — the modified repo files belong to the kernel-builder that owns the GPU lock).

**Key design decisions, all grounded in the actual code:**

- **Compile-time config (§1):** a `ParConfig&lt;DP,TP,PP,SP,ZeROStage&gt;` template (SP kept expressible, `static_assert`'d to 1). The megakernel gains a `class Par = par::SingleGPU` default param; all comm is `if constexpr (Par::kEmitComm)`-gated so `&lt;Opt, SingleGPU&gt;` is **byte-identical** to today's kernel — enforced by a PTX-diff gate. Chose a template param over a TU macro because both single- and multi-GPU points must coexist in one fat binary.

- **The cut seam (§2):** the existing `bar.sync_reset` at B2 (`fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:551`) is *already* the owner's "clean cut after the grad reduce-scatter." Under ZeRO≥2 the kernel does `if constexpr (Par::kShardOptGrad) return;` after B2; a new tiny `sharded_optimizer_kernel&lt;Opt&gt;` consumes the reduce-scattered grad. ZeRO≤1 keeps full in-kernel fusion.

- **Optimizer-shard taxonomy (§0.4, §2.4):** elementwise (adamw/lion/grokfast/neuralgrok) shard trivially; prodigy/grokadamw/looksam are elementwise **+ a tiny fixed-order all-reduce** of their global scalars (d-estimate / grad-norm / ‖g‖); **muon is per-MATRIX, SG11/SG15/SG2 are per-TENSOR** → they force a **tensor-granular shard** (the existing `_even_partition` splits mid-tensor and would break them — a gap in the current `distributed.py`).

- **Honest increment path &amp; risk:** ZeRO-3 option (a) full-pre-gather first (no mem win but correct); options (b)/(c) analyzed — (b) is incompatible with the barrier-free-across-layers P1, (c) NVSHMEM-gather is frontier. PP likely descoped at ≤13B. **Device-initiated NVSHMEM TP is the LAST increment with an explicit go/no-go gate**; host-NCCL TP (`all_reduce_tp` already exists) is the always-available fallback so the flagship ships as 4D regardless.

**Two structural wins found in the existing code:** the acts-to-HBM `DecActs` design makes PP's fwd/bwd-split a natural cut, and the persistent `state_cache` makes the step CUDA-graph-capturable with no buffer-lifetime redesign.

**Maximized pre-window work (§7):** template-instantiation compile tests, sharded-opt-vs-fused-P3 bit-parity, graph capture, and the cross-rank A/A/A determinism gate are **all 1-GPU/CPU-authorable now** (multi-rank-one-GPU NCCL loopback covers DP=2). Only NVSHMEM-TP and weak-scaling measurement genuinely need the 8×H100 window — so the rental window stays short.

I also reconciled the doc with the **pre-existing Stage-7 layer** (`distributed.py`, `megakernel_engine.py`, `test_3d_parallel.py`): it's reused for the rank mesh / ZeRO-3 collectives / NCCL bootstrap, but is insufficient alone (it shards a framework-managed model with separate fwd/bwd/step; our fused kernel needs the reduce-scatter on the kernel's `grad[total]`, the per-tensor shard mode, graph capture, and NVSHMEM — all specified as the new deltas).</result>

### ASSISTANT [2026-06-12T07:37:35]
**On ViT × SP — it's actually the *best* SP candidate of the three, not the odd one out:**

- **ViT (seq=17):** cleanest case. Encoder-style *bidirectional* attention is exactly what sequence/context-parallel schemes handle most naturally (no causal-mask asymmetry), Megatron-style SP (shard LN/activations along seq, paired with TP) applies directly — and crucially, **vit is the one model whose sequence *grows* under the planned dataset scaling** (multi-digit MNIST numbers for p>100 → more patches → seq 17 → 25+). If any model ever gives SP something real to do, it's vit.
- **Decoder (seq=4):** mechanically fine (ring-attention with causal masking is standard), but 4 tokens is nothing to shard — SP stays pointless there unless the task format gets longer expressions.
- **Mamba (seq=8):** the *hardest*. The selective scan is a sequential recurrence along seq — sharding it means chunked scan + a cross-rank prefix handoff on the block states (doable in principle because the recurrence is associative, but it's real research-grade work, not plumbing).

So the honest ranking is vit ≻ decoder ≻ mamba for SP-readiness — the reverse of the intuition. Today, at seq 4/17/8, SP has nothing to bite on for *any* of them (the axis stays in the template, pinned 1); it activates only if/when the dataset rework grows sequences, and vit is where that happens first.

**On the H100 — keep it, and here's the honest accounting:**

The 0.49% finding just made the single-GPU hill-climb the main event of the whole campaign — and that loop is *inherently* GPU-resident (propose → recompile → **measure** → keep/revert, every iteration needs the device). Still on the 1×H100 queue: the hill-climb itself (the big one), vit/mamba ladder codegen + de-pinning the d=128-pinned tail constants, the functional gauntlet, batch-knee sweeps, and the 1-GPU distributed pre-tests (sharded-opt parity, graph capture, DP=2 loopback). # Report — Task #13 opening move: loop infra + d=1024 phase diagnosis

Both deliverables landed; production stays **33/33 L3-TC green on a fresh `_ops` rebuild** (`wiring_check --require-all`, exit 0). Committed as `79d3840` on `claude/h100-audit-maximal`. ncu was unavailable (`ERR_NVGPUCTRPERM` — perf counters blocked in this container; `nsys` not installed), so the diagnosis rests on the in-kernel clock64 phase timers, which the directive ranked as the cheapest decisive instrument.

## Deliverable A — coexisting d-scaled bench-variant build

- `csrc/fused/sm_90/decoder_layout.cuh` now carries **both** layouts under one include guard, gated by `SG_DEC_BENCH_LAYOUT`: unset → d=128 (production `#else` branch, byte-identical to history, `static_assert`s fold to 422755), `=1` → d=1024. Generator `grokking_optimizers/megakernel_codegen.py` emits both (parametric `_decoder_param_sizes(d)`, `_DEC_BENCH_D=1024`); committed header == `--decoder-layout` output (no drift verified).
- `tuning/decoder_bench.py` JIT-builds the decoder TC cell TU (`mega_decoder_real_adamw_tc.cu`, already excluded from `_ops`) as a **separately-named extension with its own build dir** → ninja incremental rebuild; **verified to coexist with production `_ops` in one process**; second iteration rebuilt in **0.2 s** (no-work). It gates the dead fp32 scalar megakernel off (`-DSG_DEC_SCALAR_MEGAKERNEL=0`) for d≥768 (its `DecSampleSmem` blows the 48 KB cap — exactly why HEAD 555c0bc added the flag).
- Runbook at **`/workspace/.hillclimb_loop.md`** (edit shared kernel header → `python tuning/decoder_bench.py --d 1024 --B 16384 --reps 3` → optional `--profile`).

## Deliverable B — phase-level time breakdown (medians of 3 reps, H100, 1.98 GHz)

Un-perturbed d=1024 B=16384: **2090.9 ms/step, 0.4783 steps/s, 4.739 TF/s = 0.48% of the 989 TF/s bf16 dense roofline** — matches the task's 4.865 TF/s/0.49%/2 s premise (my analytic GEMM FLOP count = 9.909 TFLOP/step). Profile build wall 2087 ms (&lt;0.2% perturbation).

| phase | d=1024 B=16384 | d=1024 B=2048 | d=128 B=16384 |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1_fwd | 22.1% (525 ms) | 18.4% | 8.7% |
| P1_bwd | **24.0% (572 ms)** | 20.3% | 8.8% |
| B1 barrier | 12.4% (296 ms) | **41.3% (260 ms)** | 6.6% |
| P2_dW_GEMM | 18.2% (434 ms) | 7.8% | 6.9% |
| P2_grad_asm | 21.9% (521 ms) | 10.4% | **65.6% (83 ms)** |
| P3_opt_tail | 0.4% | 1.5% | 0.1% |
| B2 / B0 barrier | 0.9% / ~0% | 0.2% / ~0% | 3.2% / ~0% |
| **grid barriers Σ** | **13.4%** | **41.6%** | 9.8% |

(Slots are per-phase max-across-CTAs = critical path; summed slightly exceeds wall because different CTAs are critical in different phases. The split **does shift with B**, as asked.)

## Top-3 hypotheses for the 0.48%, ranked by evidence

1. **Per-CTA GEMM runs at &lt;1% tensor-core MFU — serialized wgmma (occupancy=1 ceiling).** Strongest evidence: at d=1024 the GEMM phases (P1_fwd+P1_bwd+P2_dW = **64.3%**) are the bulk, yet total MFU is 0.48%; and P2_dW has 12,352 split-K work items over 132 SMs (94 waves — *not* SM-starved), so its inefficiency is intrinsic per-CTA, not tiling. The ptxas **C7515** warning fires on every build: *"wgmma.mma_async serialized due to non-wgmma instructions defining accumulator registers between start/end of the pipeline stage"* — the wgmma pipeline never overlaps. Persistent megakernel = 1 CTA/SM (occupancy=1), so there's no second warp-group to hide wgmma latency. This is the **highest-leverage** lever.
2. **P2 grad-assembly is an O(V·d·T) brute-force embedding scan that should be O(d·T).** `dectc_embed_owner_scan` (csrc/fused/sm_90/model_stage_decoder_tc.cuh:1295) has each of ~99 owner-CTAs *linearly scan all T tokens with a branch* (`if tok_ids[t]==r`) and uncoalesced strided `dh0` reads — re-reading the entire activation V times. It **dominates at d=128 (65.6%)** and is still **21.9% at d=1024** (should be ~0). A scatter-add / sort-segment-reduce kills the V× redundancy and the load imbalance (only 99/132 CTAs work; threads&gt;d idle).
3. **Grid-barrier serialization from P1 load imbalance.** Barriers are 13.4% at B=16384 but **41.6% at B=2048**, where n_tiles=64 &lt; nCTA=132 → ~half the SMs do zero P1 tiles and spin at B1. The 6-barrier-per-step rendezvous (B0/B1/B2 + opt sub-barriers) serializes on the slowest CTA; exposed badly at small B, structural at all B.

## Single highest-leverage first optimization candidate

**Fix the per-CTA wgmma GEMM pipeline so the tensor core actually overlaps (hypothesis 1).** It owns 64% of d=1024 wall and the whole kernel is pinned at occupancy=1, so the C7515 serialization is paid with no latency hiding — the largest single bucket and the root of the "0.49% at every d." Concretely: restructure the wgmma accumulator lifetime so no non-wgmma instruction writes the accumulator regs mid-pipeline (clear C7515), and/or add a producer/consumer warp-specialized smem pipeline (`tile_pipeline.cuh` is already included) so the single resident CTA hides wgmma/TMA latency. The embed-scan rewrite (hypothesis 2) is a cheaper, lower-risk *parallel* win (recovers ~22% at d=1024, ~66% at d=128) and is the obvious second move; barrier/load-balance (hypothesis 3) is third. No optimization edits were made — diagnosis only.</result>

## Summary

Fixed all 3 sm_90 autotuner defects (#12 prep) in two files. **Committed: `84db23f`** (`84db23fe306fdfe961f7406db7b9b3b93247db35`) — exactly 2 files, 619 insertions / 42 deletions. The kernel-builder's 3 `csrc/fused/sm_90/*` files (concurrently edited in their lane) remain unstaged and untouched.

### Defect 1 — GEMM-blind winner-timer (HARD BLOCKER)
**Root cause confirmed by reading the code**: the autotuner builds the variant `.so` by linking the *dispatch* cells `mega_&lt;model&gt;_&lt;opt&gt;.cu` (which `#include fused_megakernel.cuh`, the L1/L3 **surrogate**, and reference **no** tile macro). The real-TC TUs `mega_&lt;model&gt;_real_adamw_tc.cu` (which pull in `model_stage_*_tc.cuh` where the tile macros live) are *excluded* from `_ops` by `_owns_extension_module_tu`. So the worker's `opt.step()` timing is structurally blind to every tile/GEMM dim.

**Fix** (`compile.py`): added a real-TC-step timer that mirrors `tuning/roofline.py` (`_tc_inputs`/`_time_tc_kernel`) and `tuning/decoder_bench.py` (`build_variant`/`measure`):
- `_time_megakernel_tc_step` (`compile.py:13179`) — builds the model's real-TC TU **with the config's tuned tile macros** (+`-DSG_TUNED_GEMM_IMPL=1` to select the wgmma driver) as a coexisting per-signature extension, then median-times `mod.tc_train_step` (warmup → CUDA-sync → N synced replays → median), returning a worker-shaped `{"timing_ms",...,"timer":"megakernel-tc"}`.
- Helpers: `_build_tc_real_step_module` (`:13100`), `_tc_real_step_inputs`, `_tc_relevant_device_flags`, `_canonical_tc_model` (`:13045`), `_config_touches_gemm_tile_dim` (`:13056`), `_GEMM_TILE_DIM_NAMES` (`:13041`).
- **Wired in at `compile.py:13673`** (`timer` closure): when the config varies a tile dim AND the model is TC-capable, use the real-TC timer; **else fall through** to the unchanged `opt.step()` worker/one-shot path. Elementwise dims keep the cheap correct timer; build/launch failure → graceful `None` → cheap timer (so a no-TC host degrades to old behaviour).

### Defect 2 — missing tile dims (`_sm90_full_space` `compile.py:1927`, `_LIVE_TUNING_DIMS` `:1274`)
READ-ONLY grep of `csrc/fused/sm_90/*.cuh` confirmed which macros are **genuinely consumed**:
| Dim added | Macro | Consumed by | #ifndef default |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile_m` [128,64,256] | `SG_TUNED_TILE_M` | decoder_tc (kSeq=4), mamba_tc (kSeq=8) | 128/64 |
| `tile_n` [128,64,256] | `SG_TUNED_TILE_N` | decoder/vit/mamba _tc (shared substrate) | 128 |
| `dec_dw_splitk` [4,1,2,8] | `SG_TUNED_DEC_DW_SPLITK` | decoder_tc | 4 |
| `vit_dw_splitk` [4,1,2,8] | `SG_TUNED_VIT_DW_SPLITK` | vit_tc | 4 |
| `mb_dw_splitk` [4,1,2,8] | `SG_TUNED_MB_DW_SPLITK` | mamba_tc | 4 |

First value = in-header default (byte-identical untuned build). Added to `_LIVE_TUNING_DIMS` floor (they also auto-detect LIVE via `_kernel_source_macros()`). Prefilter rules `tile_m/tile_n % 64 == 0` (`:1980`) mirror the wgmma-atom `static_assert`.

**Macros I SKIPPED (with reasons):**
- **`SG_TUNED_VIT_TILE_M`** — vit uses a *distinct* macro whose `static_assert` requires a multiple of LCM(kSeq=17, 64)=**1088**; {64,128,256} would all fail to compile. Only one legal value → not sweepable. (vit still sweeps the shared `SG_TUNED_TILE_N`.)
- **`mega_block`/`grad_tile`** — deliberately **not** routed to the TC timer: `SG_TUNED_MEGA_BLOCK` sets the *scalar* megakernel's `__launch_bounds__` only; the TC megakernel hardcodes `SG_TC_MEGA_BLOCK=256` and reads neither, so the TC timer is no more sensitive to them than the cheap one (verified in `fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh`). `SG_TUNED_DEC_GEMM_STAGES` is TC-read but I added no `gemm_stages` dim (out of scope).

The producer→consumer handoff is automatic: `_live_macro_map` already derives `{dim:macro}` from the live space, so the new dims flow into `export_winner`'s `_macros` map for `setup.py`.

**Cardinality recalibration (same file, `compile.py:17326` + `:17462`)**: the 5 live dims grow sm_90a's all-live Cartesian product 576× (4.2e9 → 2.42e12). Two self-test dead-inflation guards asserted `&lt; 10^10`; recalibrated to `10^13` — admits the all-live space (2.42e12) while still tripping on a dead-pin regression (~3.9e14). Recalibration, not suppression (no coverage removed).

### Defect 3 — per-model schema (`_tuned_inject.py`)
Schema is now `{arch: {model: {optimizer: combo}}}` (was flat `{arch: {optimizer}}`, last-wins across models):
- `export_winner` (`:648`) writes nested, and **migrates an old-flat block in place** (`:625`) moving each flat entry under its `model` provenance.
- `source_extra_nvcc_flags` (`:319`) gained an optional `model` param; `_lookup_combo` (`:283`) reads **both** shapes (`_arch_block_is_nested` `:263` discriminates); `model=None` → deterministic-first model (shared launcher TUs).
- `model_for_source` (`:213`) parses `mega_&lt;model&gt;_&lt;opt&gt;.cu` → short model key; `compute_source_flags` (`:460`) routes each cell to its model's winner.
- `compile.py`'s `_export_kernel_tuned_json` caller already passes `model` positionally — producer side needed no change.

<result>Everything is committed and verified. All deliverables are complete. Let me provide the final report.

## Summary

**KEPT CHANGE — M-atom-interleaved wgmma GEMM pipeline** in the shared `dectc` GEMM helper. Commit **`627d134`** (`627d1340e143292e931909aaf54fbab2c3193a7b`) on branch `claude/h100-audit-maximal` (HEAD was 79d3840, now a4ba49f via other landed work; 79d3840 is an ancestor).

**What it does** (`csrc/fused/sm_90/model_stage_decoder_tc.cuh` `tc_gemm_block_unpipelined`, + smem sizing in `fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh` `DecTcSmem`, + selftest arena in `decoder_tc_selftest.cu`): the K-chain used to run each stacked m64 atom to completion serially, re-staging the shared B (weight) tile **once per atom** and waiting after every wgmma. Now atoms run in groups of `kIL = min(MaxAtomsM, 2)`; within a group each K-step issues the 2 atom-wgmmas back-to-back into **independent fp32 fragments sharing one staged B-tile**. This (1) lets the tensor pipe overlap the two independent MMAs, and (2) **halves the HBM weight-operand traffic** (the actual bottleneck). Capped at 2 so accumulator-reg/A-smem stay bounded (dW tiles can be 8 atoms → 4 groups of 2; dW production is 1 atom → unchanged). Ascending-k accumulation per output element is untouched → bitwise-identical.

**BEFORE / AFTER (single H100, committed decoder_bench):**
- **d=1024 B=16384: 2084.25 → 1624.74 ms/step (−22.1%); 4.754 → 6.099 TF/s (+28.3%); MFU 0.48% → 0.62%**
- Per-phase (clock64): **P1_fwd 525.6→301.9 ms (−42.6%), P1_bwd 571.7→328.6 ms (−42.5%)** — exactly the 2-atom fwd/dX GEMMs; P2_dW 433→438 ms unchanged (kIL=1 by design, confirming the mechanism). New dominant phase is P2_grad_asm (not a GEMM).
- **d=128 B=16384 (production): 107.63 → 105.82 ms/step (−1.7%, no regression)** — clean back-to-back IL=1 vs IL=2 measurement (the first "regression" reading was concurrent-build contamination).

**HARD GATES (all green):**
- d=1024 A/A/A: params + state + loss (4.595632) bit-identical ×3.
- `wiring_check --require-all`: **33/33** cells on L3-TC.
- tail-gate `adamw/decoder` + `muon/decoder` (d=128, after `_ops` rebuild): **PASS** — state vs eager m≤2.2e-7/v≤9.6e-7 (adamw), 0/0 (muon); A/A/A bit-identical.
- `test_decoder_tc.py`: **18/18** (fwd/dX/dW across shapes incl. Nout=512→8-atom/4-group, dW determinism, full-cell oracle parity).

**C7515 status: STILL PRESENT** (1×; 255 regs / **0 spills** / 17844B smem at d=1024; stack dropped 1152→640B). The win is the within-group MMA overlap + B-staging-sharing, **not** C7515 removal — the cross-k single-fragment accumulator chain is inherently dependency-serialized, and true cross-k multi-in-flight needs the cp.async producer/consumer ring (large rewrite, deferred).

**What did NOT work (reverted):**
- **Single-fragment accumulator-lifetime restructure** (technique a, peel k=0 + one live fragment): pushed the kernel to 255 regs **+ 5180B spill stores / 5204B spill loads** (worse than baseline's 0 spills), and **did not clear C7515**. SASS showed ptxas inserts a `WARPGROUP.DEPBAR.LE gsb0,0x0` after *every* HGMMA regardless — confirming the serialization is register/dependency-bound, not the indexed-array hypothesis I started with. Reverted.
- **Deepening to 3–4 smem stages with deferred waits** (technique b multi-in-flight): provably unsafe with the current synchronous (non-cp.async) staging — with one resident warp-group you can't `__syncthreads` mid-flight and can't protect a ring slot from the next stage's writes racing an in-flight wgmma read without the empty/full mbarrier handshake. Not attempted in code; the M-atom interleave captured the achievable overlap instead. (S=1 vs S=2 measured only +2.1%, which initially under-predicted the interleave — the interleave's real lever turned out to be the B-traffic halving.)</result>

Fix (decoder header only): a dW work item now owns a **group of up to `kDecDwIL=2` consecutive m64 atoms** → engine runs 2 wgmmas/k-step into independent fragments sharing ONE staged X-operand tile (overlap MMAs + 2× less X-operand HBM traffic on the K=T contraction). All decoder weights have even atom counts → split-K partial-scratch total unchanged; `DecTcSmem.sA` already had the room. Ascending-k preserved per atom → bit-exact.

| **P2_dW_GEMM** | **436.47 → 302.60 ms (−30.7%)** | 8.92 → 6.94 ms (−22.2%) |

G=8 ties @ d=128 (−0.27%, noise) but loses +2.4% @ d=1024. **No G beats G=4 at BOTH beyond noise → keep G=4** (the knob was already cross-scale-optimal). No code change, no rebuild needed.

→ Moves 2, 3, 4 = **K=3 consecutive non-improving → loop stopped.**

Remaining @ d=1024: **P1_fwd 353.7 ms (now top) + P1_bwd 346.9 + B1_barrier 242.8** — all three are now bounded by the **same root cause**: the persistent one-CTA/SM kernel pays raw wgmma/HBM latency (occupancy=1, no async producer/consumer overlap) AND the static grid-stride tile assignment leaves a 4:3 tail-wave imbalance that the B1 barrier serializes on.

**Recommend the cp.async producer/consumer mbarrier ring next** (the explicitly-deferred big rewrite): it is the single change that attacks P1_fwd, P1_bwd, AND P2_dW simultaneously (overlaps the HBM operand stream with the wgmma pipe across the persistent kernel) and a proper task queue would also dissolve the B1 imbalance. <result>Both commits are complete and clean. The task is fully done. Let me write the final deliverable report.

---

## Task #24 Phase 1 — COMPLETE

Both decoder hill-climb wins (H1 forward/dX GEMM interleave + H3 dW tile interleave) ported to the **vit** and **mamba** megakernel twins. Both landed green and committed.

### Shared-vs-duplicated (the first thing the task asked to measure, not assume)
The GEMM engine is **DUPLICATED, not shared** — neither twin inherited H1. Each model has its own `tc_gemm_block_unpipelined` in its own namespace (`vittc::`, `mbtc::`), and neither references `dectc::`. So H1 was **not** already live for either; both the engine (H1) and the dW driver (H3) had to be ported per-model. Critically, the two twins had **diverged differently** from the decoder:
- **mamba** kept the decoder's exact structure (S-stage ring + `stage_k`/`issue_k` lambdas + `acc[MaxAtomsM]`) → near-verbatim port (`dectc`→`mbtc` renames).
- **vit** had been rewritten to a **single reused accumulator with no ring** — because vit's `kAtomsM=17` made `acc[17]` spill to local memory (C7515). So vit was a port to a structurally *different* (unpipelined) engine, not a copy.

### Per-model before/after (single H100, production B=16384, median of 5; new `tuning/{vit,mamba}_bench.py` mirroring `decoder_bench.py`)
| Model | HEAD baseline | H1+H3 | Δ | serial-refactor (interleave=1) A/B |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **vit** | 166.59 ms | **123.58 ms** | **−25.8%** | 146.43 ms (interleave itself −15.6%) |
| **mamba** | 108.03 ms | **102.70 ms** | **−4.9%** | 105.65 ms (interleave itself −2.8%) |

vit far exceeded the expected "modest" gain because the interleave **eliminated the engine's register spills** (see below) on top of the MMA-overlap/operand-sharing win. (mamba's TILE_M=128→kAtomsM=2, so its fwd/dX *do* interleave — not the no-op I first suspected before checking.)

### ptxas reg/spill deltas (`fused_*_megakernel_tc`, OptId 0 = AdamW)
| | vit baseline | vit after | mamba baseline | mamba after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Registers | 255 | 255 | 255 | **255 (held)** |
| Spill stores | **6452 B** | **0 B** | 40 B | 40 B |
| Spill loads | 6676 B | 0 B | 108 B | 96 B |
| Stack | 2704 B | 672 B | 1424 B | 2224 B |
| smem | 7664 B | 9712 B | 14800 B | 18896 B |

**Mamba's 255-reg/occ-1 zero-margin caveat held** — kIL=2, occupancy-1 preserved (the GridBarrier launcher did not refuse), spills unchanged, **no fallback to kIL=1 needed**. The register-pressure-sensitive prodigy/mamba and supergrok2/mamba cells still pass A/A/A. Even-atom assumption: verified per-weight; mamba's **x_proj (Nout=40 → 1 atom, ODD)** is handled by the ragged-tail logic (the one weight the decoder's all-even shortcut would have broken) — it cost one extra 64×N split-K slot, documented in-source. vit weights are all even.

### Gate outputs (raw, all after production `_ops` rebuild)
- **wiring_check --require-all: 33/33** (decoder 11/11, vit 11/11, mamba 11/11, all `L3-TC-megakernel(wgmma)`).
- **Tail-gates (the L3-TC cell parity + A/A/A, with the REAL optimizers):**
  - `adamw/vit` → PASS: Δp 2.4e-7, m 2.0e-7/v 9.5e-7; **A/A/A loss+grad+param bit-identical**.

### What I kept / reverted, and why
**Nothing reverted.** Both ports kept.

The one honest caveat: **`test_mamba_tc.py` is 3-fail/2-pass — but PRE-EXISTING at HEAD, not a regression.** I verified by checking out HEAD's mamba headers, rebuilding, and rerunning: `test_tc_single_step_grad_parity`, `test_tc_proj_dw_exact_on_own_operands`, and `test_tc_determinism` **already fail on unmodified 276861d**. Cause: the JIT-TU's out_proj-L1 dW is in the bf16 noise floor (ref.max ~5.6e-4 → rel~1.0), and the JIT-TU A/A/A grad race at B=64 is the known mamba register-pressure condition that `__noinline__ mbtc_backward_tile` mitigates but doesn't fully close. **My port reproduces the identical 3-fail/2-pass set** — neither fixes nor worsens it. The *shipped* path (production `_ops` L3-TC) A/A/A **is** green (the mamba tail-gates above prove it). This matches HARDWARE_VALIDATION.md already marking the L3 megacells 🟡 numeric-unvalidated.

# Measurement + validation bundle — RESULTS

Single H100 80GB, HEAD f560b89, no kernel edits. All three jobs ran on the production `_ops.so` (untouched throughout — mtime 11:44, predates this session).

## JOB 1 — #19a multistep parity gates

**Verdict: 1 TEST-BUG (fixed test-side, committed separately) + both gates now PASS. NOT a kernel bug.**

First GPU run: `grokadamw` **passed**, `prodigy` **failed** at assert (b) — the vacuity guard `d_grew_step is not None` (eager `_d_lr` never grew off d0=1e-6).

Root cause (TEST-BUG): the controlled-anchor `delta` was **sign-random**. Prodigy's d-candidate is `d_coef·r_ema/|s_ema|` with `r_ema ∝ &lt;g, p0−p&gt; = &lt;g, delta&gt;`, and `d = max(d_prev, candidate)` only moves when that inner product is **positive**. For the decoder's actual step-1 reduced grad the random delta gave `&lt;g,delta&gt; ≈ −3.6e-3` (negative) → candidate negative → d pinned at d0.

Proof it's the test, not the kernel — the offline reference probe `tuning/_prodigy_owner_block_unit.py` (which the test was derived from) **also fails on both decoder AND vit** for the identical reason, yet its in-kernel persisted `(r_ema, s_ema, d_lr)` matches the fp64 canonical `prodigy.h` reference to **r_rel ~1e-6 / s_rel ~1e-7 / d_rel ~1e-9** (tol 5e-4). Only "d MOVED" is False. The kernel d-update arithmetic is correct.

Fix (test-side only, commit `3555ff9`): build a reproducible magnitude `delta_mag` and align its sign with the step-1 reduced grad at injection time so `&lt;g, delta&gt; = Σ|g|·|δ| &gt; 0` strongly (≈+1.0). Result: `d_grew_step=2` (d: 1e-6 → 1.50e-2), `worst_d=1.6e-6`, `final_param=1.3e-6`, `ctl_div=1.04` — all four asserts pass; kernel-vs-eager parity unaffected. Re-run: **2 passed**. (The sibling sign-random bug in `_prodigy_owner_block_unit.py` is noted in-comment; left as-is since it's a probe, not the gate.)

File: `tests/hw/test_multistep_parity.py` (only file changed; +23/−1).

## JOB 2 — #16 batch-knee sweep (no OOM at any B)

**Decoder** (`--d 1024` bench layout, 25.4M params, TF/s vs 989 roofline):

| B | wall/step | steps/s | TF/s | roof% |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2048 | 199.9 ms | 5.003 | 6.196 | 0.63% |
| 4096 | 244.2 ms | 4.096 | **10.146** | 1.03% |
| 8192 | 484.1 ms | 2.066 | 10.234 | 1.03% |
| 16384 | 967.2 ms | 1.034 | 10.245 | 1.04% |
| 32768 | 2076.3 ms | 0.482 | 9.545 | 0.97% |
| 65536 | 4030.2 ms | 0.248 | 9.835 | 0.99% |

**Decoder knee = B≈4096** (TF/s jumps 6.2→10.15 and stays within ~1% through 16384, then declines slightly past it).

**ViT** (production d=128, 418K params) and **Mamba** (mamba3_layout, 259K) — these benches emit steps/s + wall only (no FLOPs/TF-s). Throughput is samples/s = B × steps/s:

| Model | B=4096 | B=16384 | B=65536 |
|---|---|---|---|
| ViT steps/s | 19.66 | 8.06 | 1.98 |
| ViT samples/s | 80.5K | 132.1K | 129.6K |
| Mamba steps/s | 39.72 | 9.73 | 2.49 |
| Mamba samples/s | 162.7K | 159.4K | 163.4K |

**ViT knee ≈ B=16384** (samples/s saturates 132K→130K from 16384→65536). **Mamba knee ≈ B≤4096** (samples/s flat ~160K-163K across all three — already saturated by 4096; from 4096→16384 steps/s drops the full ~4×).

## JOB 3 — #18 post-wins roofline (the AFTER table)

Full **33/33 cells** ran (`--models decoder,vit,mamba`, bf16-forced, B=16384), wall **1669s ≈ 28 min** — under the 75-min budget, so **no cells skipped**. All 33 fire `L3-TC-megakernel(wgmma)` (l3_engine=wgmma, path_family=tc, no ABI-stale degradation). Per-cell wall ranged ~8s (decoder/adamw, 30 st/s) to ~180s (the 3 supergrok2 cells, 1.2-1.3 st/s, the cost drivers).

Achieved TF/s + roof% (vs the engine's bf16 989-TF ceiling):

| optimizer | decoder TF/s · roof% | vit TF/s · roof% | mamba TF/s · roof% |
|---|---|---|---|
| adamw | 4.85 · 1.70 | 5.63 · 1.81 | 2.01 · 0.82 |
| neuralgrok | 2.45 · 0.84 | 4.30 · 1.34 | 0.71 · 0.28 |
| grokadamw | 4.86 · 1.68 | 5.85 · 1.85 | 2.00 · 0.81 |
| supergrok | 5.60 · 1.10 | 7.20 · 1.29 | 1.70 · 0.39 |
| supergrok15 | 4.20 · 1.29 | 5.10 · 1.43 | 1.57 · 0.56 |
| supergrok2 | 0.22 · 0.07 | 0.92 · 0.28 | 0.24 · 0.09 |
| grokfast | 4.80 · 1.69 | 5.62 · 1.81 | 2.01 · 0.82 |
| muon | 4.29 · 1.47 | 5.44 · 1.74 | 1.94 · 0.78 |
| lion | 4.81 · 1.69 | 5.63 · 1.81 | 2.01 · 0.82 |
| looksam | 4.43 · 1.30 | 5.12 · 1.38 | 1.89 · 0.65 |
| prodigy | 4.80 · 1.69 | 5.77 · 1.85 | 1.99 · 0.82 |
| **mean** | **4.12 · 1.32%** | **5.14 · 1.51%** | **1.64 · 0.62%** |

**ALL-33: mean roof% = 1.150%, median = 1.293%, mean 3.64 TF/s.**

BEFORE comparison: the exact "0.48-0.49%" figure isn't reproduced by any single archived baseline JSON — the available baselines are eager/native-batch `roofline_eager_baseline_20260610` (mean 0.384%, median 0.155%) and bf16/native-batch `roofline_bf16_contended_1922` (mean 0.920%, median 0.571%). Against the eager baseline the AFTER mean is **2.99×** (decoder 4.89×, vit 1.96×, mamba 5.42×). Caveat: BEFORE baselines use native batch + the pre-wiring eager path, so it's not strictly apples-to-apples with the AFTER bf16/B=16384/L3-TC run — the AFTER table stands on its own as the post-wins evidence.

(The roofline harness wrote `results/h100_grokking_race/roofline.json` + `.png` by design; I extracted the data and then `git checkout --` restored both tracked artifacts to their committed state.)

## Summary

All three deliverables implemented, RUN for real on the single H100, and committed (`ef433ac` on top of `7656ea6`). Production `_ops` untouched — `wiring_check --require-all` = **33/33 L3-TC**. Tree clean except the 4 new files.

### DELIVERABLE 1 — `tests/hw/test_sharded_optimizer.py` (+ `tests/hw/sharded_optimizer_binding.cu`), §7.3
At DP=1, `sharded_optimizer_kernel&lt;Opt&gt;` over the full param set is **BIT-IDENTICAL** to the in-kernel P3 fused tail. Used the task's equivalent check: production fused step (`return_grad=True`) → re-apply via the JIT-bound sharded kernel on the captured grad + zero state + the same `_opt_scalars_from` scalars. JIT-bound via `cpp_extension.load` into `/workspace/.torch_ext/sharded_opt_bind` (own build dir; production build never touched). Elementwise ids {adamw, lion, grokfast} × {decoder, vit, mamba}:
```
9 passed — every cell params bit-eq=True maxd=0.000e+00 | m_eq=True v_eq=True | grad_unmutated=True
```
**No loosening needed** — the assert is `torch.equal`, not a tol. Reason it's exact (not ULP): the kernel includes `opt_components.cuh` verbatim and calls the same `apply_optimizer&lt;Opt&gt;`; `rebase_state` only offsets per-element pointers, so flat-shard index `i` pairs the identical `(params[i], grad[i], m[i], v[i], ema[i])` + identical scalars as the per-tensor P3. The grokfast step-1 `ema[idx]=grad[idx]` cold-start replicates too.

### DELIVERABLE 2 — `tests/hw/test_dp2_loopback_determinism.py`, §7.1 + §6.4
`torchrun --nproc_per_node=2`, both ranks on `cuda:0`. NCCL 2.20.5 rejects two ranks on one device by default ("Duplicate GPU detected … ncclInvalidUsage") — defeated with a distinct `NCCL_HOSTID` per rank (the documented loopback workaround; verified `all_gather`/`reduce_scatter` then work). Per rank: production fused step on its batch shard → **fixed-order** grad reduce (`all_gather` + ascending-rank fp32 sum — the §2.7/§6.4 A/A/A non-negotiable, not raw NCCL reduce-scatter) → sharded-opt (DELIVERABLE-1 kernel) on the owned shard → `all_gather` params.
```
4 passed:
adamw/decoder:   (a) cross-rank bit-eq=True | (b) A/A/A bit-eq=True | (c) maxd=2.689e-05 rel=6.614e-06 (tol 3e-05) parity=True
lion/decoder:    (a) True | (b) True | (c) maxd=0.000e+00 rel=0.000e+00 parity=True
grokfast/decoder:(a) True | (b) True | (c) maxd=4.447e-05 rel=1.094e-05 parity=True
adamw/vit:       (a) True | (b) True | (c) maxd=4.414e-05 rel=1.178e-05 parity=True
```
(a) and (b) are **bit-exact**; (c) is within parity tol as the design predicts (DDP-equivalence: averaging two half-batch grads = the full-batch mean grad up to bf16 reduction grouping). The actual deltas are reported in-test.

**One non-obvious fix, documented in-file:** the eager DP=2 loopback initially *hung* — a rank busy-spinning in an NCCL collective starves the peer's full-GPU persistent megakernel on the shared device. Fixed by keeping per-rank device work **symmetric** (both ranks compute the (c) reference) + an explicit `torch.cuda.synchronize()` between every megakernel and every collective + `NCCL_BLOCKING_WAIT=1`. Workers then tear down cleanly (no kill).

### DELIVERABLE 3 — `tests/hw/test_step_graph_capture.py`, §7.4
Captured `[ops.fused_step → sharded_optimizer_kernel]` as **one CUDA graph** on rank 0's stream, replayed 5×:
```
3 passed — every replay (adamw/lion/grokfast, decoder): bit-eq-to-eager=True maxd=0.000e+00 (N=5)
```
**Honest what-was/wasn't-captured (documented in-file):** the persistent megakernel captures fine (it's one launch). I verified per-piece that `fused-step`, `sharded-opt`, the combined no-collective chain, AND a standalone NCCL `all_gather` in the loopback all capture+replay. What is NOT robustly capturable on this 1-GPU loopback is **mixing the full-GPU megakernel with NCCL collectives across two ranks on one device** — a CUDA graph can't host the inter-op `synchronize()`s the eager loopback needed to dodge the megakernel-vs-collective SM contention. That mixed cross-rank capture is the genuine 8-GPU-window activity (one rank per device removes the contention), per §7.5. Shipped the §7.4-endorsed `[fused-step → sharded-opt]` fallback.

<result>The repository is in a clean, verified state at f78a9e2 with no tracked changes. The untracked files listed are all pre-existing (they were present before I started — confirmed against the initial `git status` at the top of the session). My work created no new repo files.

The task is complete with the disciplined outcome: clean revert + precise blocker report. Let me write up my findings.

---

## ENDGAME task #13 — OUTCOME: clean revert + blocker report

**Result:** Reverted to f78a9e2 cleanly (`git diff` empty, HEAD=f78a9e2, `wiring_check --require-all` = **33/33** green: decoder 11/11, vit 11/11, mamba 11/11). No commit. No half-landed state. Honest documented failure, per directive.

### What I built and verified
1. **Read the full campaign evidence + engine** (`model_stage_decoder_tc.cuh::tc_gemm_block_unpipelined`, the `DecTcSmem` arena, all fwd/dX/dW call sites, the H1 commit 627d134, and the existing substrate primitives).
2. **Discovered the cp.async/mbarrier infrastructure already exists** but is unwired into the decoder: `csrc/backends/cuda/sm_90/tile_pipeline.cuh` (`TilePipeline&lt;N,Depth&gt;`, `pipeline_produce_ktile`, `tc_pipelined_gemm_m64nNk16`) + `warp_specialize.cuh` (`Mbarrier::arrive_expect_tx/try_wait/init`, `setmaxnreg`) + `primitives.cuh` (`cp_async_cg_16/ca_4/commit/wait_group`). It is silicon-validated only in `wgmma_selftest.cu`, never in the megakernel.
3. **Established the live baseline** (after fixing a blocker, see below): **968.5 ms / 10.23 TF/s / 1.03% MFU** at d=1024 B=16384 (3 reps: 969.0/968.5/967.6), matching the prompt's ~967.3 ms. Profile (clock64 critical path): **P1_fwd 27.0% + P1_bwd 29.4% + P2_dW_GEMM 26.8% + B1_barrier 14.4%** — the three GEMM phases are ~83% of summed cycles.

### BLOCKER 1 — the target bench is unrunnable at HEAD (pre-existing regression, NOT the ring)
`python tuning/decoder_bench.py --d 1024 --B 16384` **OOMs** at the workspace allocation (`torch.OutOfMemoryError: Tried to allocate 191.97 GiB`), before any kernel runs. Root cause: commit **7656ea6** ("de-pin the d=128 SuperGrok2 Nmax constants", task #24, 13:51Z Jun 12 — *after* all three task-13 H-moves at 08:15–10:45Z) re-derived `kDecSG2Nmax` from the layout table → 4d²=4.19M at d=1024 → `dec_sg2_ws_stride_floats` ≈ 377M floats/CTA × 132 CTAs ≈ 199 GB. This is unconditionally carved even for AdamW (which never touches it). The limit is *self-documented* at `fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh:443-455`. The H1/H2/H3 baselines were measured *before* 7656ea6 when Nmax was pinned at 65536, so the bench fit then.

**Fix found and empirically verified** (this is what unblocked the 968.5 ms measurement): gate the four staged-opt scratch regions (Prodigy/Muon/LookSAM/SG2) to 0 floats at bench width via `#if SG_DEC_BENCH_LAYOUT` → `constexpr bool kDecStagedOptScratch=false`, with the kernel's `embed_ws` offset derived from the same `dec_tc_staged_opt_floats(nCTA)` aggregate so host carve and kernel offsets stay byte-consistent. **Production (d=128, flag unset) is byte-identical** (`kDecStagedOptScratch=true`). This is a ~35-line, production-invisible change in `csrc/fused/sm_90/fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh` (two functions + one kernel line). I reverted it per "revert EVERYTHING," but the next session should **re-apply it first** (2 min) to measure — without it the entire d=1024/132-CTA hill-climb cannot run. Exact diff is in my working notes; the touch points are `dec_tc_workspace_floats` (lines ~469) and the `embed_ws` computation (line ~584).

### BLOCKER 2 — the cp.async/mbarrier ring is structurally blocked on THIS engine (the real finding)
Three independent, hard obstacles, all matching the task's flagged candidates:

- **(a) fp32 weights converted on read — the dominant operand can't be cp.async'd.** `dectc_gemm_fwd`/`dectc_gemm_dx_f32` source the B (weight) operand as `__float2bfloat16(W[...])` from the **fp32** param blob (intentional: `model_stage_decoder_tc.cuh:427-431` "needs NO bf16 weight buffer"). cp.async/TMA copy raw bytes and **cannot convert fp32→bf16**. In fwd the weight tile (N×16=2048 bf16, read as 4096 B fp32) is the *larger* operand and the dominant HBM traffic — and it's exactly the one that must stay synchronous. The A operand (X/dY, contiguous bf16, 1024 elems) is cp.async-able, but async-ing only the smaller operand while the larger fp32 weight load stays synchronous has a theoretical ceiling of ~2-3% at d=1024 (likely within the ±0.2% noise band → fails "faster beyond noise"). Making weights async needs bf16 weights pre-staged in HBM = a new conversion phase + cross-CTA buffer + a barrier → **explicitly out of scope** ("the existing GridBarrier/phase structure stays untouched").

- **(b) the dW phase (26.8%) has BOTH operands transposed-strided.** `dectc_dw_run_tile` reads `srcA=dY[k*Nout+m]` and `srcB=X[k*Kin+nn]` — both strided in the contraction index k, so neither is 16-byte contiguous. cp.async needs contiguous chunks; this needs a **TMA descriptor with transpose**, which the design (`tile_pipeline.cuh:199`) makes **explicitly phase-2** — the "TMA descriptor plumbing" blocker the task named.

- **(c) the engine is ALREADY at the 255-register ceiling WITH spills — no headroom for mbarrier state.** `build.log` shows the shared `tc_gemm_block_unpipelined` (mamba/decoder/vit all use it) compiling at **"Used 255 registers" with "2636 bytes spill stores, 2672 bytes spill loads."** Adding mbarrier ring state (per-slot parity bits, ring indices, barrier addresses, fp32-prestage convert state) costs more registers on a kernel already spilling at the ceiling → more spills (defeats the latency win) or, with the grown smem, risks **occupancy=0 → GridBarrier hang** (the catastrophic mode the task warns of). The task's mitigation ("move indices to smem") helps marginally but the kernel has no register slack to begin with. This is the "register pressure" blocker, present *before* any ring code.

### Why I did not land a partial ring
The only in-scope variant (cp.async the contiguous A operand in fwd/dX, weights synchronous) has a best-case win below the noise floor (obstacle a) and touches the shared 255-reg-spilling engine (obstacle c), so it cannot clear "faster beyond noise + no d=128 regression + 33/33 + tail-gates + 18/18 + A/A/A" within the box with acceptable risk. The effective variant (async weights via fp32→smem prestage) fits smem at S=2 (33KB &lt; 48KB) but adds the most register pressure to the already-spilling engine and would rebuild every production d=128 cell, risking their occupancy=1/hang-freedom — un-verifiable green in the remaining window.

### Recommended path for the next session (start from knowledge)
1. **Land the BLOCKER-1 OOM fix as its own green commit first** (production-invisible, unblocks all d=1024 measurement). Then 968.5 ms is the live baseline.
2. **Attack obstacle (c) before the ring**: the engine spilling at 255 regs is the true gating constraint. Reduce register pressure (e.g. the kDecMuonMaxNumel-driven stack, or move the dW spec/ring indices to smem) to open headroom — *then* the mbarrier ring becomes feasible.
3. **For the ring itself, the only structurally-sound version** is: pre-stage bf16 weights into HBM once per step (a small new P0.5 phase, which DOES require touching the phase/barrier structure — escalate that scope), OR commit to TMA descriptors per orientation (the phase-2 path) so the transposed dW operands and fp32 weights both stream async. Both are multi-hour structural efforts, not a single-session hill-climb on the current engine.

Key files: `csrc/fused/sm_90/model_stage_decoder_tc.cuh` (engine + wrappers), `csrc/fused/sm_90/fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh` (workspace sizing + kernel offsets, the OOM fix site), `csrc/backends/cuda/sm_90/tile_pipeline.cuh` (the unwired mbarrier ring), `tuning/decoder_bench.py` (the d=1024 harness), `/workspace/SuperGrok1.5/build.log` (the 255-reg-spill evidence).</result>

## Summary

Re-implemented the BLOCKER-1 fix cleanly: gated the decoder's four staged-opt scratch regions off at bench width so `decoder_bench --d 1024` no longer OOMs, while keeping the production path byte-identical. Committed green.

**File changed:** `/workspace/SuperGrok1.5/csrc/fused/sm_90/fused_decoder_megakernel.cuh` (54 insertions, 4 deletions; ~35 lines of substance, rest comments).

**The fix (3 touch points, per the directive):**
1. Added `constexpr bool kDecStagedOptScratch` — `false` under `#if SG_DEC_BENCH_LAYOUT`, `true` otherwise.
2. Gated the four staged-opt helpers (`dec_tc_opt_reduce_floats`/Prodigy, `dec_tc_muon_floats`/Muon, `dec_tc_looksam_floats`/LookSAM, `dec_tc_sg2_floats`/SG2) to `return 0` when `!kDecStagedOptScratch`; introduced a single `dec_tc_staged_opt_floats(nCTA)` aggregate and routed `dec_tc_workspace_floats` through it.
3. Derived the kernel's `embed_ws` offset from that SAME aggregate at bench width (`#if SG_DEC_BENCH_LAYOUT` branch); the production `#else` branch keeps the original `sg2_ws_base + dec_tc_sg2_floats(nCTA)` chain verbatim (the SG2 int64 align bumps are load-bearing). Documented the OOM rationale + honest-scoping note (references 7656ea6/campaign notes) in comments.

**Key implementation finding:** The `.o` byte-compare is NOT a valid byte-identity oracle here — two no-change HEAD builds differ in embedded fatbin metadata (char 730239). The faithful oracle is `cuobjdump -sass` (perfectly reproducible). I used that.

**Raw gate outputs:**

- **Gate 1 — bench runs, no OOM, ~968.5 ms:**
